# Route WD goal loop (Opus, 2026-10-09)

- **ID:** wd-goal-loop-2026-10-09
- **Stage:** 1-in-progress (working log for the route-WD goal; one row per cycle)
- **Goal:** develop route WD (frontier map §WD) toward a Lean-formalized unconditional RH proof.

## Frontier at start (from the Lean corpus)

- Receivers, all [L]: collision charge `O(T) < 2 ⟺ RH` (`XiHorizontalCollisionChargeEndpoint`);
  Ward edge energy `0` or `> 8π` (`XiWardEdgeEnergyPhaseQuantizationUniversalityLaw`); slice
  positivity `c_{2k}(t) ≥ 0` for `k < (¼+ε) log t` (`XiSliceCoeffPositivitySharpWindow`); per-zero
  cone criterion (`XiHorizontalSliceConeCriterion`); first-Laguerre two-way law (`XiLaguerreSeparation`).
- Densities, all [L]: all-order slice positivity on `≥ 18/25` of heights (moment `7/50`); horizontal
  isolation `0.6725`; singleton fibres `≈ 0.628`; Lamzouri lane saturated (H15).
- Producers: every WD Need is an RH-strength source estimate (cap modules, two-prime carrier, Ward
  energy all say so in their docstrings). No non-RH-strength pointwise WD statement is open.

## Programme: single-height RH criterion (genus-0 Edrei–ASW by deflation)

Target: RH ⟺ the coefficients `b` of `1/Ξ(√y)` (or of `1/(Ξ(t+σ)Ξ(t−σ))` at one height) have all consecutive-row Toeplitz minors `≥ 0`.
- A. Bordered limit (HTGeometry): if `b_{N+1}/b_N → ρ`, then consecutive-row `m`-minors of `b' = b − ρ·shift b` are limits of `(m+1)`-minors of `b` with an extra far column `N`, divided by `b_{N−i₀−m}`. Hence consecutive-row TP passes to `b'`.
- B. Ratio limit: log-concave positive coefficients of a function analytic on `|y|<D` with a pole on `|y| = D` have `b_{N+1}/b_N → 1/D`.
- C. Step: the minimum-modulus law gives the pole at `+D`. `G = dslope F (−D)` has reciprocal coefficients `D·(b − b'/D)`; A and B give TP of the new reciprocal.
- D. Induction on the zero count in the closed disk of radius `|y₀|`: every zero is negative real. A non-real pair on a circle is excluded once the positive pole there is exhausted.
- E. ξ: the centre function `F(y) = ξ(½+√y)` as an entire power series; RH ⟹ reciprocal PF∞ via geometric factors; the converse via D.
- Later: Jacobi complementary minors to state it for the slice coefficients themselves.
| 7 | Single-height RH receiver by deflation (genus-free Edrei–ASW zero location). If `1/F(−z)` has nonnegative, consecutive-row totally positive Taylor coefficients, every zero of the entire `F` is negative real. For ξ: TP of the coefficients of `1/Ξ(√z)` ⟹ RH. | PROVED (sufficiency). The converse (RH ⟹ reciprocal TP via geometric factors) is next. | `HTGeometry/ToeplitzBorderedDeflation` (`tendsto_toeplitzMinor_bordered`, `ConsecutiveRowTP.deflate`); `HTGeometry/LogConcaveRatioRadius` (`tendsto_ratio_of_logConcave`); `HTGeometry/ReciprocalTotalPositivityDeflation` (`ReciprocalTP.apply_neg_eq_zero`, `ReciprocalTP.deflate`); `HTGeometry/ReciprocalTotalPositivityRealZeros` (`ReciprocalTP.eq_neg_of_apply_eq_zero`); `HTGeometry/EvenEntireSquareLift`; `RHTower/XiReciprocalCentreTotalPositivity` (`riemannHypothesis_of_reciprocalTP_xiCentreLift`). | 96 | Source-side sufficient conditions for coefficient positivity. (a) g_t is the cosh-transform of a positive measure, i.e. the coefficients form a Hamburger moment sequence (Hankel PSD). (b) CIRCULAR. p_k(t) ≥ 0 for all k means Σ_ρ(t−γ_ρ)^{−2k} ≥ 0 for all k, whose large-k limit is dominated by the nearest zero, so off-line nearest zeros give sign oscillation (row 42(a)). As a function of y it is the Laplace transform of the SIGNED measure Σ_k U_k(cos θ_p)q₀^k δ_{k log p}, so no local positivity holds at generic heights. Even with positive local factors, the product converges only for x > ½ and its expansion at x₀ > ½ has radius < x₀ (Landau, row 68), never reaching x = 0. | — | The Euler product is a product, but of signed local slice factors (Chebyshev phases at height t), and only on the Euler half-plane. The zero product is a product of positive factors (under RH), but on the critical side. WD positivity needs the second, and the source supplies only the first. The FE bridges the two only through the full analytic continuation, which is the whole problem. |

| 102 | Sign from topology: Descartes–Laguerre for the slice. In y = x², g_t(y) = Σ_j c_{2j}(t)y^j is entire with c₀ > 0 and eventually positive coefficients (sharp window). For real x, g_t = |ξ(½+x+it)|² ≥ 0, so every positive y-zero (y = δ² for an off-line zero at height t) has even multiplicity, and both end signs are positive. Hence V(t) is always even and parity carries nothing. The Laguerre bound V(t) ≥ #positive zeros ≥ 2·(off-line multiplicity at height t) gives a quantized invariant: an off-line zero forces V ≥ 2, and RH ⟺ V(t) < 2 ∀t ⟺ V ≡ 0. Coefficient-positive series are closed under products and locally uniform limits, so reciprocal positivity holds at t if there is an injection φ from off-line conjugate pairs to distinct critical copies with (t − γ_{φ(z)})² < (√2−1)\|t − z\|². At scales 1/log t ≲ R ≲ T^{0.33} no local proportion is known, and at R ≲ δ Hall asks for a critical zero within 0.644δ of every off-line ordinate, which near-line off-line zeros violate generically. Hall at all scales and heights ⟺ RH-strength. | — | Reciprocal positivity reduces to a scale-by-scale counting inequality between off-line pairs and critical zeros (Hall form). | 113 | Deformation with quantized invariants. Ward energy ∈ {0} ∪ (8π, ∞) (`xiWardEdgeQuadEnergy_arithmeticPhaseClassification` [L]) and collision charge ∈ {0, 1} ∪ [2, ∞) are integer-like. By FE symmetry a SIMPLE critical zero cannot leave the line under small symmetric perturbations, so off-line zeros are born only from multiple critical zeros. Conjecture: some path F_s from an LP function to Ξ (heat flow H_λ, λ: 1/8 → 0; convex combinations; Euler truncations completed) has no multiple critical zero, so the invariant stays 0 and RH follows. | CIRCULAR / FALSE per path (paper). Convex combinations of FE-symmetric functions: Davenport–Heilbronn-type functions show FE + symmetry allow off-line zeros, so collisions occur on generic paths. Euler truncations: no FE, no symmetry, so the line-rigidity mechanism is absent. A collision-free path must carry Euler structure AND FE symmetry, and none is known. | — | Quantized receivers make RH a no-collision statement along any symmetric deformation: off-line zeros arise only at multiple critical zeros. Hence 1 − h_{n+1} + \|b\|²h_n < 0 for some n (the nearest-zero law, corpus `XiReciprocalSliceNearestZero`). Excluding only critical/off-line coincidences is strictly weaker than RH. Even if proved, it would turn reciprocal coefficient positivity into a criterion, not supply it. | 120 | Classify finite even real configurations whose reciprocal slices are coefficient-positive at every admissible height. Conjecture: exactly those in which every off-line pair z, z̄ has a real zero at its exact ordinate Re z (single suffices, row 110 [L]; double, corpus octic). | FALSE as stated; partial picture (paper). Necessity fails in general: the Voronoi condition only asks that some zero be nearer than z at every real t, and real zeros straddling Re z at distances < δ can achieve that without one at Re z. Whether low-order coefficients then stay positive depends on the ½-threshold geometry (row 109) for each matching. For a SINGLE real companion, exactness is necessary (row 115 [L]); for two companions both must be nearer at every t, which forces both to Re z (bisector argument). | — | The exact-ordinate configurations are the canonical blind ones; the general classification is a finite Voronoi/threshold geometry problem with no bearing on the source side. Detection lane is closed; no further WD detection cycles planned. |
| 121 | Order-1 WD (c₂ = Ξ′² − ΞΞ″ ≥ 0) only fails near near-line off-line zeros. At height t, c₂/c₀ = Σ_z Re(t−z)^{−2}: an off-line pair contributes ≥ −2/\|t−z\|² ≥ −2/δ², while the critical zeros contribute Σ_crit (t−γ)^{−2} ≳ (log t)² at typical heights. So failure needs δ ≲ C/log t and \|t − γ_ρ\| < δ. Conjecture: with the corpus proportion ≥ 0.6725 of simple critical zeros, the order-1 bad set has density → 0. | FALSE as a density-one statement (paper). The constant C is set by the local critical-zero sum, which is controlled only on average (bandwidth wall again). Order-1 density gains are constant-level, like row 104. | — | Even the weakest WD statement (order 1) needs pointwise control of near-line off-line zeros against local critical-zero sums. | 122 | Euler-using pointwise input near σ = ½ of the only available kind: large values. Resonance/large-value theorems give heights with \|ζ(½+it)\| ≥ exp(c√(log t · log log log t / log log t)). By Jensen there are no zeros within distance ≍ log\|ζ(½+it)\|/log t of such t, so Ξ(t)² dominates M(R) there and the shadowing depth bound (row 31 [L]) caps the order of any negative coefficient. Conjecture: WD holds at all large-value heights, and large-value heights are dense enough to propagate. | INSIGNIFICANT / no propagation (paper). Large-value heights are good heights, but the corpus already has unconditional good open intervals above every height and positive density of all-order positivity (rows 14/15, 3/4 density [L]). Positivity at a set of heights does not propagate to its complement: the bad set is open (row 23), and nothing in the large-value method controls heights between resonance points. Large values are existence statements (Ω-results), never universal lower bounds. | — | Every Euler-using pointwise input near σ = ½ known (large values, CLT, resonance) is existential or distributional. WD needs a universal pointwise statement. This confirms the empty cell of the row-83 map from the input side. |

Conjecture: applied at s = ½ + x + it with the truncation T tied to x, it yields slice-coefficient positivity. | BRIDGE to route HB; no WD producer (paper). The Maass–Selberg positivity controls \|φ(s)\| = \|ξ(2s−1)/ξ(2s)\| only where the oscillatory term is dominated, i.e. σ near 1. That gives zero-freeness of ζ(2s) near Re 2s = 1 (the classical edge), mirroring the quarter-log edge (row 103). Pushing it toward σ = ½ is the open Eisenstein/Maass–Selberg sign problem catalogued in the HB lane (memory hb-route-hub). The truncated norm is positive but its Taylor coefficients in x are not, so no slice-coefficient positivity follows. | — | The Eisenstein reading turns WD into HB's Maass–Selberg sign laws; it inherits HB's wall at the σ = 1 edge. All spectral positivity available (Maass–Selberg, Rankin–Selberg) lives at the edge of absolute convergence. |
| 124 | Stability-preserver producer (Pólya–Schur). Ξ(t) = Σ(−1)^n b_n t^{2n}/(2n)! with moments b_n = ∫u^{2n}Φ(u)du > 0. If (b_n) were a multiplier sequence, applying it to cos t (coefficients 1) would give Ξ ∈ LP, hence RH and every WD positivity at once. Conjecture: (b_n) is a multiplier sequence, i.e. F(x) = Σ b_n x^n/n! = ∫Φ(u)e^{xu²}du ∈ LP⁺. | FALSE (paper, elementary). F is entire (Φ decays like exp(−πe^{2\|u\|})) and F(x) = ∫Φ(u)e^{xu²}du > 0 for EVERY real x (positive integrand), so F has no real zeros. An LP⁺ function without zeros is c·e^{ax}, but log F is not linear (F(x) ≥ ∫_{\|u\|≥U}Φe^{xu²} grows faster than any e^{ax}, since its log grows like x·U² for every U). So (b_n) is not a multiplier sequence. Variants b_n = γ_n a_n with (a_n) the coefficients of another LP function require Σ(b_n/a_n)x^n/n! ∈ LP⁺: a factorization of the moment sequence that is Pólya's open problem in another form. | — | Pólya–Schur/Borcea–Brändén producers need Ξ = T(f) with f real-rooted and T a stability preserver. The natural T (the moment multiplier) fails for a sign reason: the moment generating function of a positive kernel is zero-free on ℝ. The backward heat flow, the other natural T, is not a preserver (row 91). |
| 125 | Refined Pólya–Schur producer: compare with Pólya's real-rooted ξ* (kernel Φ*(u) = 2π²(e^{9u/2}+e^{−9u/2})exp(−π(e^{2u}+e^{−2u})), moments a_n). If γ_n = b_n/a_n is a multiplier sequence, then Ξ(√z) = Σ(−1)^n γ_n a_n z^n/(2n)! is obtained from the real-rooted Ξ*(√z), giving RH. | FALSE, by a rigidity lemma (paper). (i) b_n/a_n → 1: the moments concentrate at u_n → ∞ (u_n ~ ½log n), where Φ/Φ* = 1 + O(e^{−2u}). (ii) Lemma: a multiplier sequence with γ_n → 1 is identically 1. G(x) = Σγ_n x^n/n! ∈ LP⁺ means G = c·e^{σx}Π(1 + x/x_k) with Σ1/x_k < ∞. But G(x)e^{−x} = E[γ_N] with N ~ Poisson(x), which → 1 as x → +∞. Since log Π(1 + x/x_k) = o(x), this forces σ = 1, an empty product and c = 1, i.e. G = e^x. (iii) b_n ≠ a_n (Φ ≠ Φ*), so (b_n/a_n) is not a multiplier sequence. | — | Multiplier rigidity: a real-rooted comparison function whose moments are asymptotic to Ξ's cannot be carried to Ξ by any multiplier sequence. Pólya–Schur producers would need a comparison function with different moment asymptotics, i.e. a different growth class, and then the quotient grows or decays geometrically. That is Pólya's original problem with no slack. Not formalized: needs the Laguerre–Pólya representation theorem, absent from the corpus. |
| 126 | Remaining Pólya–Schur slack after row 125: multiplier sequences that do not tend to 1. (a) Factorial: γ_n = 1/n! is a multiplier sequence (Σxⁿ/(n!)² = I₀(2√x) ∈ LP⁺), so f₁(t) = Σ(−1)ⁿ b_n n! t^{2n}/(2n)! ∈ LP would give RH. (b) Polynomial: γ_n = Π(n + r_i), r_i ≥ 0, so Ξ = P(θ)f with f = P(θ)^{−1}Ξ, a dilation average of Ξ (θ = z d/dz, z = t²). Conjecture: some such f is provably in LP. | BOTH FAIL (paper). (a) b_n ≍ (½log n)^{2n}·…, so f₁ has coefficients ≍ exp(−(m/2)log m + m log log m) in m = 2n: order 2 and infinite type, which excludes LP (LP functions of order 2 are e^{−at²} times genus ≤ 1). Every factorial multiplier overshoots the growth class. (b) f = ∫₀¹Ξ(t√s)s^{r−1}ds is a dilation average, and real-rootedness of f is no easier than that of Ξ. The averaging operator is not even LP-preserving (1/(n+r) is not a multiplier sequence: Σxⁿ/((n+r)n!) = ∫₀¹e^{sx}s^{r−1}ds > 0 on ℝ, the row-124 sign obstruction). | — | Pólya–Schur producers are exhausted: γ_n → 1 is rigid (row 125), factorial multipliers break the growth class, polynomial multipliers push the problem onto an equally hard average of Ξ. Combined with rows 91/124, every stability-preserver route reduces to real-rootedness of a transform of Ξ that is as hard as RH. |
| 127 | Axiomatic shortcut: is there a class of Dirichlet series between "FE + symmetry" (Davenport–Heilbronn-type off-line zeros exist) and ζ itself for which WD is provable from the axioms alone (Euler product + FE + Ramanujan bound, Selberg class S)? Conjecture: degree-1 Selberg-class axioms imply all slice coefficients ≥ 0. | CIRCULAR (paper). By the Kaczorowski–Perelli classification, degree-1 members of S are exactly ζ and shifted primitive Dirichlet L-functions, so "WD for degree 1 S" = GRH for Dirichlet L-functions, strictly stronger than RH. Weaker axiom sets (dropping the Euler product) admit Davenport–Heilbronn countermodels, and keeping only Euler product + FE without the Ramanujan bound leaves the same classification problem. No axiom set strictly between is known to force real zeros. In the function-field case the extra input is geometry (Frobenius on H¹, row 88), absent from S. | — | No axiomatic route: the minimal class carrying an Euler product and FE is already the arithmetic class whose RH is the target. Any producer must use an input beyond the analytic axioms (a cohomological or spectral structure), which lies outside WD (rows 88/89/100). |

The lower bound Σ_mixed 2√(2/3)δ_ρ ≤ \|bad set\| ≤ 2μT only gives Σ_mixed δ_ρ ≤ √(3/2)μT, weaker than the off-line moment bound Σ_all δ_ρ ≤ μT already in the corpus (`ZetaLocalWindowTailUpper` [L]). The Lamzouri loss is a COUNT of mixed points (each costs ≥ 3 − 2√2 deficit regardless of δ), while slice measures weight each mixed class by δ. Near-line mixed classes (δ → 0) are cheap in measure and expensive in count. Counting them is the pair-correlation problem beyond \|α\| = 1 (row 112). | — | Measure-type (slice) information weights mixed classes by δ, while count-type (Lamzouri) losses do not. The two cannot be traded without control of near-line coincidences, the same pointwise near-σ = ½ input. |

Conjecture: shrink the window to H = T^{1/2+ε} (Montgomery–Vaughan mean values with AFE length √T, mollifier length T^{21/50}), or further to H = C/log T, giving a good height in every short interval. | H = T^{1/2+ε}: PLAUSIBLE but INSIGNIFICANT (exponent bookkeeping; the 24/25 comes from the taper 2a^{19/20} and block lower bound a ≥ U^{49/50}, and an MV-type window bound would remove it). H = C/log T: OUT OF REACH. A good height in every interval of length C/log T needs local control of off-line zeros at the scale of the zero spacing, i.e. pointwise near-σ = ½ information. | 130 | No-slack principle for WD producers. Conjecture (meta): WD at λ = 0 could follow from an open condition, a positivity margin stable under small perturbations of the source (heat time λ, kernel Φ, Euler factors), via compactness or perturbation. | REFUTED as a proof strategy (paper, from corpus [L]). By Newman–Rodgers–Tao (`riemannHeat_neg_nonreal_zeros_infinite`), for every λ < 0 the deformation H_λ has infinitely many non-real zeros, so WD fails for H_λ at infinitely many heights. Any condition implying WD that is open in λ would also hold for some λ < 0: a contradiction. So every WD producer must be closed/rigid in the heat parameter, true at λ = 0 and false for all λ < 0. The same holds for kernel perturbations Φ ↦ Φ + εψ that move zeros off the line (Davenport–Heilbronn-type), so the producer must use an exact identity not preserved by generic perturbation. | — | Meta-constraint, now explicit: WD (equivalently RH) is "barely true" (Λ = 0 at best). Producers must exploit exact arithmetic structure (Euler-product exactness, FE, functoriality) with zero slack. That excludes every margin-based analytic method tried in rows 36–129 (density, averaging, dominance, mean values) as standalone producers. |

| 132 | Inventory of exact (slack-free, row 131) identities available to a WD producer, and their WD readings. Conjecture: one of them, combined with the slice structure, is rigid enough to force WD. | NONE SUFFICES (paper; consolidates rows 69/79/84/97/98/123). (1) Euler product: exact only for Re s > 1, with signed local slice factors (row 97). (2) FE / theta modularity: exact everywhere but symmetric, so it gives the slice structure itself and no sign (Davenport–Heilbronn countermodels). (3) Explicit formula: exact, but linear in the zero measure (power sums p_k), while WD is the Newton-polynomial (elementary-symmetric) positivity e_j(p₁,…,p_j) ≥ 0 (rows 69/96). Linear identities cannot certify product positivity without the full Weil cone (row 90). (4) Rankin–Selberg / Maass–Selberg positivity: exact quadratic positivity, but only at the edge of absolute convergence (row 123). | — | WD needs an exact product-type positivity near σ = ½. Even if m_j(s) ≥ 0 held, it is a single global average per (j, s), dominated by the lowest zeros (Ξ² decays), so it lies in the averaged column and carries no pointwise information at large heights. | — | Bernstein–Widder closes the polyharmonic route: the only fully signed test functions are cosh-transforms, which turn the identities into global low-height averages. | 139 | Truncation producer (Hurwitz). Ξ_L(z) = 2∫₀^L Φ(u)cos(zu)du → Ξ locally uniformly. If every Ξ_L (or a sequence L_n → ∞) has only real zeros, Hurwitz gives RH. Pólya-type finite-interval theorems (increasing kernels ⇒ real zeros) suggest it, and Φ is decreasing on (0, L). | FALSE, by zero counting (paper; heuristic constants, not formalized). Ξ_L has exponential type L, so its large-\|z\| zeros have density 2L/π (boundary term 2Φ(L)sin(zL)/z, real zeros). Below the transition z* ≈ 4e^{2L} (where \|Ξ\| ≈ e^{−πz/4} meets Φ(L)/z ≈ e^{−πe^{2L}}) its real zeros follow Ξ, with density (1/π)log(z/2πe) < 2L/π. Jensen on \|z\| = R gives ∫n(r)dr/r = (2L/π)R − log R + O(1). An all-real zero set gives (2L/π)R − c·z* log R + O(1) with c ≈ (1/π)log(2πe/4) ≈ 0.46. So ≈ c·z* ≈ 1.85e^{2L} zeros must be non-real. Decreasing step kernels already give non-real zeros: (1−ε)sin(z/2) + ε sin z, ε < 1/3. | — | Truncation creates a frequency/density mismatch at the transition scale, forcing about e^{2L} non-real zeros. Hurwitz approximation from below by finite-interval transforms cannot work. Real-rootedness must come from the whole kernel (de Bruijn's universal-factor theorems need the full exp(−a cosh u) tail). |
| 140 | Whole-kernel structure (follow-up to row 139). Φ(u) = Σ_n φ_n(u) with φ_n(u) = (2π²n⁴e^{9u/2} − 3πn²e^{5u/2})e^{−πn²e^{2u}}, and φ_n is (a weighted copy of) φ₁ translated by −log n. So Φ = μ * φ₁ with the positive discrete measure μ = Σ_n w_n δ_{−log n} (after symmetrization), and φ̂₁ is real-rooted (Pólya: the Bessel/Macdonald-type transform of e^{−a cosh u}). Conjecture: convolution with this positive measure preserves real zeros, giving RH. | CIRCULAR (paper). Convolving kernels multiplies transforms: Ξ = φ̂₁·μ̂, and μ̂(z) = Σ_n w_n n^{iz} is the Dirichlet series, i.e. ζ itself up to the Γ-factor. Convolution preserves real-rootedness only for Pólya-frequency kernels, whose transforms are reciprocals of LP functions and so zero-free. μ̂ has zeros (the zeta zeros), so μ is not PF∞, and real-rootedness of φ̂₁μ̂ is exactly that of μ̂ on the relevant line: RH. | — | The kernel splits as (real-rooted archimedean part) * (arithmetic positive measure). Every zero of Ξ is a zero of the arithmetic factor, and positivity of the measure (positive Dirichlet coefficients) does not control the zeros of its transform. This is the archimedean/arithmetic factorization, with all difficulty in the arithmetic factor. |

| 145 | Landau positivity route. Any smoothing ΣΛ(n)w(n) − Σw(n) keeps both signs, since ψ(x) − x changes sign infinitely often (Littlewood; corpus abscissa/signed dictionary memories). More generally, D = G/ζ with d ≥ 0 forces G = ζH, H ≥ 0, hence D = H with no forced singularities at the zeros. | 155 | Sum version of the slice: the cosine shift A_h(t) = ½(Ξ(t+ih) + Ξ(t−ih)) = cos(hD)Ξ pairs the same two values Ξ(t ± ih) as the WD slice g_t(h) = Ξ(t−ih)Ξ(t+ih), by sum instead of product. Conjecture: real-rootedness of A_h for small h (a sum-type WD) is easier than product positivity and transfers to WD. | REDISCOVERED (internal, searched: `XiCosineShiftLaguerrePolya`). The corpus proves A_h ∈ LP whenever every zero has \|Re ρ − ½\| < h (Lagarias–Suzuki factor comparison), unconditionally for h ≥ ½, and for all h > 0 under RH. So A_h ∈ LP at small h is a zero-free-strip statement, the sum-form analogue of row 142's off-centre WD: the Euler edge again for h ≥ ½, open below. No transfer to products: real-rootedness of the sum does not give coefficient positivity of the product (they are different functionals of the same pair). | — | Sum (cosine shift) and product (WD slice) functionals of Ξ(t ± ih) have the same unconditional range: the zero-free strip. Both reduce to zero-free regions, and the corpus already owns the cosine-shift side. |
| 156 | Hermite–Biehler family. For real t, h: g_t(h) = \|E_h(t)\|² with E_h(z) = Ξ(z + ih) = A_h(z) + iB_h(z) (A_h the cosine shift of row 155, B_h the sine shift). E_h is Hermite–Biehler (no zeros in Im z > 0) iff every zero has Im γ < h, which is unconditional for h ≥ ½. Conjecture: the de Branges structure of the family {E_h}, h ≥ ½, propagates to WD (coefficient positivity of h ↦ \|E_h(t)\|² at h = 0) by continuity in h. | NO SLACK / REDISCOVERED (rows 99, 131; corpus `XiHermiteBiehlerCount`, `StrictHermiteBiehlerGivesRH`). The HB property of E_h for every h > 0 is equivalent to RH (zeros with Im γ < h for all h > 0). HB is an open condition in h only from above: it holds on (h₀, ∞) with h₀ = max Im γ, and propagating it down to h = 0⁺ is the zero-free-strip problem. At h = 0 the family degenerates (E₀ = Ξ real on ℝ), and WD at h = 0 is the boundary statement, rigid in the sense of row 131. | — | WD is the h → 0⁺ boundary of the de Branges family {Ξ(· + ih)}. The family is HB unconditionally exactly for h ≥ ½ (Euler edge), and moving the threshold to 0 is RH. Same boundary as rows 142/155. |
| 157 | Interpolate the edge threshold (rows 142/155/156): let h*(T) = max{\|Re ρ − ½\| : 0 < Im ρ ≤ T}, so RH ⟺ h* ≡ 0, and unconditionally h*(T) ≤ ½ − c(log T)^{−2/3}(log log T)^{−1/3} (VK). Conjecture: h* obeys a convexity or Phragmén–Lindelöf principle in an auxiliary complex parameter (e.g. the count N⁺(h) of zeros of E_h = Ξ(·+ih) in the upper half-plane, as a function of complex h), forcing h* ≡ 0 from its behaviour at h ≥ ½. | FAILS (paper). N⁺(h) is integer-valued and jumps exactly at h = Im γ, so it has no analytic interpolation, and integer-valued functions admit no Phragmén–Lindelöf principle. h*(T) is a running maximum of the zero displacements, and no convexity is known or expected: it equals 0 or jumps to the first off-line displacement. Analytic quantities in h (log\|E_h\| averages, Jensen integrals) are subharmonic and give upper bounds, which by row 151 do not propagate inward. | — | Threshold functions of the edge are integer- or max-valued (quantized/receiver type, rows 102/112/113). They carry no analytic structure that could interpolate from h ≥ ½ to h = 0. |
| 158 | Deformation from a world where WD is a theorem (function fields, row 88) to ℚ. Conjecture: a family connecting ζ_{F_q(T)}-type zeta functions (WD true by Weil) to ζ, e.g. through q → 1 limits (F_1 heuristics) or Drinfeld-module analogues, carries WD across by continuity of slice coefficients and the no-collision principle (row 113). | FAILS (paper; corpus F₁ no-go). No such family exists as a continuous family of entire functions: function-field zetas are rational in q^{−s} (finitely many zeros, periodic in t), while ζ has infinitely many non-periodic zeros, so a continuous interpolation must create infinitely many zeros, and creating them is exactly where collisions and off-line births can occur (row 113). The q → 1 limits are formal (F₁), and the corpus F₁ forced-shape no-go (ULF-F1-003) excludes the finite integral lattice such a limit would need. Continuity of WD under such a limit would also contradict the no-slack principle unless the limit is rigid (row 131). | — | No bridge from Weil's theorem to ζ: different zero-counting regimes (finite periodic vs infinite non-periodic), with births of zeros forced in between. |
| 159 | Small values of \|ζ(½+it)\| as WD input. Selberg's central limit theorem makes log\|ζ(½+it)\|/√(½ log log t) asymptotically Gaussian, so very small values are rare in measure. An off-line pair near the line at height γ forces c₀ = Ξ² small nearby relative to c₂ (near-double law, row 24). Conjecture: Gaussian control of small values excludes near-line off-line pairs at almost every height, or at every height via a maximal inequality. | AVERAGED COLUMN (paper). Selberg's CLT (and its extensions to small values, Soundararajan-type) holds in measure: it bounds \|{t ∈ [T,2T] : \|ζ(½+it)\| < e^{−V√(log log T)}}\| by Gaussian tails. That is a density statement, the same column as rows 104/121 (WD density < 1 from proportions). A maximal (every-height) version would need pointwise lower bounds on \|ζ(½+it)\| away from zeros, which do not exist (row 101: pointwise inputs are upper bounds only). Small values also occur near critical zeros, so they cannot distinguish off-line pairs. | — | Distributional theorems (CLT, large/small value distributions) live in the averaged column. Small \|ζ\| is caused by critical zeros as much as by off-line ones, so even pointwise small-value control would not isolate the WD obstruction. |
| 160 | Positivity on ℝ plus evenness as a criterion. For real x, g_t(x) = \|ξ(½+x+it)\|² ≥ 0 and g_t is even, entire of order 1. Conjecture: a nonnegative even entire function of order ≤ 1 on ℝ whose real zeros are all double and lie only at x = ±δ_ρ has positive Taylor coefficients in x² once its "complex zero part" is controlled by the FE. | FALSE (paper, elementary). Nonnegativity on ℝ does not force coefficient positivity: (1 − x²)² = 1 − 2x² + x⁴ ≥ 0. The double real zeros of g_t at x = ±δ (off-line zeros at height t) are exactly what produces negative coefficients (per-height exclusion, row 49). Under RH g_t has no real zeros, its complex zeros sit at x = ±i(t − γ), and positivity is the elementary-symmetric positivity of the weights (row 96). The FE gives only the evenness already used. | — | Positivity of g_t on ℝ is automatic (\|·\|²) and carries no information. WD lives in the location of the complex x-zeros (imaginary axis ⟺ RH), so evenness + nonnegativity on ℝ is the trivial part of the structure. |
| 161 | Two-height Gram positivity of slices. For heights t₁, …, t_m and real x, the matrix [Ξ(t_a − ix)Ξ(t_b + ix)]_{a,b} is PSD (Gram of the vectors Ξ(t_a + ix)). Conjecture: the Taylor-coefficient matrices in x of this Gram family, beyond row 58(a)'s K_{2j}, have a positive structure (e.g. the coefficient of x^{2j} is a PSD combination of shifted Gram matrices), giving WD at each t_a. | REDISCOVERED / FAILS (row 58(a)). Gram positivity holds for each fixed x, but coefficient extraction in x does not preserve PSD: the x^{2j} coefficient matrix is Σ_{k}(−1)^k·(Gram of derivatives), which is indefinite in general. Its diagonal is the slice coefficient c_{2j}(t_a) itself, so PSD of the coefficient matrix would contain WD. | — | Multi-height Gram structure is positivity in x-values, not in x-coefficients; the value → coefficient step is the WD problem. |
| 162 | Descent to the verified range. RH is verified numerically up to height ≈ 3·10¹² (Platt–Trudgian; corpus Lean low-height results are much lower, e.g. the order-23 trace to 14.68). Conjecture: a descent principle, "an off-line zero at height T forces an off-line zero (or a WD failure) at some height ≤ f(T) with f(T) < T", lets the verified range propagate to all heights. | NO MECHANISM (paper; corpus row 92(c)). Off-line zeros are not linked across heights by any known identity. The explicit formula couples all zeros to the primes but gives no implication from a high off-line zero to a lower one, and finite exceptional sets of off-line zeros are compatible with every proved WD/explicit-formula statement (row 92(c)). Landau/Ingham-type oscillation theorems transfer an off-line zero to large oscillation of ψ(x) − x at LARGE x (ascent, not descent). | — | Verification-based routes need a descent principle; the only height-coupling (explicit formula, oscillation) works upward. No route from finite verification to all heights. |
| 163 | Global complementarity of the two slices (row 111 shows it in the mixed model: at every t ≠ γ the direct OR the reciprocal coefficients are positive). Conjecture: for the actual Ξ, at every admissible height at least one of the two slices is coefficient-positive, so their bad sets are disjoint. Since both bad sets are open and contain every off-line ordinate of the right type, this would constrain off-line zeros strongly. | FALSE in general (paper). Take an isolated off-line pair z = γ + iδ with no zero within distance δ of γ. At t = γ the direct slice fails (g_γ(δ) = 0 forces a negative coefficient, row 49), and the reciprocal slice fails too: the pair is the nearest zero and b is real negative, so the coefficients alternate at high order (nearest-zero law; explicitly `coeff_one_reciprocalPairModel_neg` in the model). Complementarity holds only in the exact mixed configuration, where the critical zero sits at γ. | — | The two slices detect complementary configurations (direct: exposed or mixed, reciprocal: Voronoi-unshadowed), but an isolated off-line pair is detected by both. Their bad sets overlap exactly on such pairs. No exclusion follows. |
| 164 | Quantitative order-1 WD as a single scalar criterion. Under RH, c₂/c₀ = Σ_γ(t−γ)^{−2} ≥ c(log t)² at all large t (critical-zero density; even the largest gaps leave a sum of order (log t)²). Conjecture: RH ⟺ c₂(t)/c₀(t) ≥ c(log t)² for all large t (with c₀ = Ξ² ≠ 0), a scalar margin version of order-1 WD that might detect shadowed pairs through the margin. | FALSE as an equivalence (paper). A far off-line pair (δ ≫ 1/log t) contributes 2Re(t−z)^{−2} ≥ −2/δ², negligible against Σ_crit ≍ (log t)², so it passes the margin test at every height. That is the finite-order insufficiency (corpus) in quantitative form: no fixed-order statement, with or without a margin, sees shadowed far pairs. Near-line pairs (δ ≲ 1/log t) violate the margin near their ordinates, but those are already detected by plain order 1 when unshadowed. | — | Margins at fixed order detect only near-line unshadowed pairs. Far pairs need orders ≍ log t (sharp window), confirming that the RH content is the growing-order block below the quarter edge. |
| 165 | Multiscale reading of the RH block (row 164). Order 2k < ½log t has saddle radius R ≈ 2k/log t ∈ (0, ½), so c_{2k}(t) probes the zeros in a disc of radius ≍ R about ½ + it. The RH block is therefore a multiscale test over R ∈ (0, ½) at every centre. Conjecture: positivity at scale R holds whenever the zero configuration in the disc of radius CR is "regular" (local count within O(1) of (R log t)/π, no off-line point closer than R/C), and regularity at all scales follows from mean-value estimates. | FAILS at small scales (paper). Regularity at scale R is a local zero-count statement in discs of radius R about ½ + it. For R ≫ T^{θ−1}·… (large discs) mean values control counts on average, but for R ≍ 1/log t (the zero spacing) local counts fluctuate by O(1) unconditionally (S(t) is O(log t) only), and "no off-line point closer than R/C" at R → 0 is the absence of near-line zeros: the pointwise problem. Mean-value estimates give regularity for most centres, never all. | — | The RH block is a multiscale local-regularity test; its small-scale end is the pointwise near-line problem, its large-scale end is the quarter edge. Mean values cover only most centres at each scale (density, rows 104/121/129). |
| 166 | Zero geometry of the slice in complex x. The zeros of g_t(x) = ξ(½+x+it)ξ(½−x+it) are x = ±(ρ − ½ − it), i.e. the zeros of ξ seen from ½ + it. RH ⟺ every zero of every g_t lies on the imaginary x-axis. On that axis g_t(iy) = Ξ(t+y)Ξ(t−y) is real. Conjecture: Jensen/argument-principle counting for g_t in x-discs, compared with sign changes of g_t(iy), forces all x-zeros onto the imaginary axis. | COUNTING / CIRCULAR (paper; rows 48/112). The x-disc count of zeros of g_t equals twice the number of zeros of ξ within that radius of ½ + it, and the sign-change count along the imaginary axis equals twice the number of critical zeros in (t − R, t + R). Equality of the two for all t, R is N₀ = N locally (Hardy–Littlewood/Selberg/Levinson counting), which unconditional methods establish only in proportion (0.6725 simple critical, corpus). | — | The complex-x geometry of the slice re-encodes the critical-zero count; WD ⟺ local N₀ = N, the counting formulation, which stops at proportions. |
| 167 | Hardy's method for slices. Hardy shows infinitely many critical zeros: the moments ∫Ξ(t)t^{2n}cos(αt)dt, as α → π/4⁻, have signs incompatible with Ξ eventually of one sign (corpus Hardy's theorem [L], Hardy census). Conjecture: the analogous moments ∫c_{2j}(t)t^{2n}cos(αt)dt of slice coefficients, controlled by the Φ-autocorrelation kernels M_j (row 77) near the boundary α → π/2, force c_{2j} ≥ 0. | WRONG DIRECTION (paper). Hardy's method proves EXISTENCE of sign changes, hence zeros on the line, from boundary behaviour of a Fourier transform. Applied to c_{2j} it would show c_{2j} changes sign or that certain moments have definite signs, never that c_{2j} is nonnegative everywhere: positivity of a function cannot be read off finitely many of its weighted moments. Under RH, c_{2j} > 0 and its boundary moments are positive, but positive moments are compatible with negative values on sets of small weight. | — | Hardy-type boundary-moment arguments produce existence (sign changes, critical zeros), the opposite logical type to WD's universality (absence of sign changes of the coefficients). |
| 168 | Quantifier map of unconditional methods (refines row 83). Classify every input used in rows 36–167 by logical type: (∃) existence of good heights or sign changes (Hardy, large values, resonance, good intervals [L]); (μ) density or measure statements (mean values, mollifiers, CLT, zero density, Husimi averages [L]); (∀-edge) universal statements at σ ≥ 1 − o(1) (Euler product, zero-free regions, Maass–Selberg); (∀-centre) universal statements at σ = ½. Conjecture: some combination of (∃), (μ), (∀-edge) yields a (∀-centre) WD statement. | NO (paper, structural). (∃) and (μ) are closed under weakening but never produce ∀: a density-one set can miss an open bad set (row 104), and existence says nothing about the complement (row 122). (∀-edge) statements propagate outward only (row 151). The bad set of WD is open (row 23), so a ∀-centre statement needs exclusion of every open bad interval, which (∃)/(μ) cannot see and (∀-edge) cannot reach. The no-slack principle (row 131 [L]) shows the ∀-centre statement is a rigid boundary property, not a limit of the others. | — | Logical summary of the WD frontier: all unconditional inputs are ∃, μ or ∀-edge, and WD is ∀-centre. No combination crosses that gap without a new rigid ∀-centre input. |
| 169 | Refinement of row 168's μ-class: exact null versus asymptotic density. Since the WD bad set is open (row 23 [L], `riemannHypothesis_iff_ae_xiSliceCoeff_nonneg`), a measure-type statement of the form "the bad set has Lebesgue measure 0" does give ∀ (a null open set is empty). Conjecture: some μ-method reaches exact nullity rather than density. | NO KNOWN μ-METHOD GIVES EXACT NULLITY (paper). Mean-value/mollifier methods bound the bad measure in windows [T, T+H] by ε(θ)·H + O(1) (row 104: ε(θ) = (1/2π)[ln(1+2θ)/(2θ) + ln(1+1/(2θ))]), with ε(θ) > 0 for every finite θ. Taking θ → ∞ is the long-mollifier problem, and even ε → 0 gives measure o(H) per window, not 0: an open set of measure o(H) in every window can still be nonempty. Exact nullity needs the bad measure to vanish identically, which no averaging method certifies. | — | Corrected logic: μ-statements at full measure would suffice (openness), but available μ-methods give only asymptotic density with positive error ε(θ). The gap is between "bad measure o(T)" and "bad measure 0": a pointwise statement in measure-theoretic form. |
| 170 | Converse density transfer. Conjecture: every off-line zero with δ ≥ 1/log γ makes the direct slice bad on an interval of length ≥ c/log γ around γ. Then the WD bad measure B(T) ≥ (c/log T)·N(½ + 1/log T; T, 2T), and together with B(T) ≤ ε(θ)T (row 104) this gives a WD-native zero-density estimate N(½ + 1/log T; T, 2T) ≤ (ε(θ)/c)·T log T. | FAILS: no uniform interval-length lower bound (paper). The bad set contains a neighbourhood (γ − η, γ + η) of each off-line ordinate (openness, row 23), but η ≥ m/D, with m = \|negative coefficient at γ\| and D a t-derivative bound. m can be arbitrarily small: near-double configurations (row 24) and shadowing (rows 26/41) make the negative coefficient tiny, and in the exact mixed model the direct bad interval is (γ − √(2/3)δ, γ + √(2/3)δ) (row 111 [L]), but other critical zeros can shrink it further. Without a uniform η the transfer gives nothing, and the resulting density estimate would anyway only match Selberg's classical N(½ + 1/log T, T) ≍ N(T)·const. | — | WD bad measure and zero density are linked only through configuration-dependent interval lengths. The transfer runs zero density → bad measure (row 104), never back. |
| 171 | Which ∀-centre inputs exist at all (row 168)? (a) The FE and reality of Ξ on ℝ: universal at σ = ½ but sign-free. (b) The Riemann–Siegel formula Z(t) = 2Σ_{n≤√(t/2π)} n^{−½}cos(θ(t) − t log n) + O(t^{−1/4}): universal at σ = ½ with an explicit remainder, and Euler-using (Dirichlet-series structure). Conjecture: RS, applied to the slice (Ξ, Ξ′, Ξ″ near t), gives c_{2j}(t) ≥ 0 at every t for bounded j. | FAILS (paper; row 84). RS is the unique ∀-centre, Euler-using analytic input, but it supplies values, not signs. WD at height t needs local control at scale 1/log t, where Z is a sum of ≍ √t oscillating terms with error t^{−1/4} ≫ \|Z\| near small values. Positivity of slice coefficients from RS is the "no negative resonance of a length-√t quadratic Dirichlet form" problem (row 84), i.e. bounded-order WD from a finite trigonometric sum with √t terms, with no known positivity mechanism. | — | The ∀-centre inputs are exactly FE/reality (sign-free) and Riemann–Siegel (value-level, oscillatory). WD's missing input is a positivity theorem for RS-type sums at every t, which is the bandwidth problem at its sharpest. |
| 172 | Positivity for Riemann–Siegel-type sums (row 171's missing input), tested at the level of the main sum. For a finite cosine sum f(t) = Σ a_n cos(λ_n t + φ_n), the order-1 WD form is f′² − ff″. For two terms it equals λ² + μ² + ½(λ+μ)²cos((λ−μ)t) + ½(λ−μ)²cos((λ+μ)t) ≥ 0 (cos λt + cos μt is real-rooted). Conjecture: the RS main sum Z_N(t) = 2Σ_{n≤N}n^{−½}cos(θ(t) − t log n) satisfies WD (all slice coefficients ≥ 0) at every t for N = ⌊√(t/2π)⌋. | FALSE beyond two terms (paper; classical). Dirichlet polynomial partial sums ζ_N(s) = Σ_{n≤N}n^{−s} have zeros off every vertical line: Montgomery shows zeros in Re s > 1 for all large N, with Re s up to 1 + (4/π − 1 − ε)log log N/log N (Montgomery–Vaughan). The completed RS main sum inherits non-real zeros in t, so its slice coefficients fail somewhere. Real-rootedness of Z comes only from the full symmetric structure (AFE with both sums plus remainder), never from the truncated main sum. | — | Truncation obstruction on the Dirichlet side, mirroring row 139 on the kernel side: finite truncations (of Φ or of Σn^{−s}) leave the LP class. A positivity theorem for RS sums must use the exact AFE symmetry between the two sums, the FE again, so it reduces to the full problem. |
| 173 | Phase-monotonicity mechanism for the symmetric AFE (row 172 says positivity must use the two-sum symmetry). Z(t) = 2Re(e^{iθ(t)}ζ_N(½+it)) + R(t); its zeros are where arg(e^{iθ}ζ_N) ≡ π/2 mod π. If the Riemann–Siegel phase θ(t) ~ (t/2)log(t/2πe) dominated arg ζ_N monotonically, Z would have exactly one zero per Gram interval (Gram's law), giving N₀ = N and WD. Conjecture: a refined phase-monotonicity (Gram blocks / Rosser's rule) holds at every height. | FALSE (classical). Gram's law fails for a positive proportion of Gram intervals (Selberg; Trudgian's explicit proportions), and Rosser's rule fails infinitely often (Lehman). arg ζ(½+it) = πS(t) + …, with S(t) unbounded (Ω((log t)^{1/3−ε}) unconditionally, Selberg/Montgomery-type), so no monotone dominance holds pointwise. Phase-counting gives N₀ ≥ cN in proportion only. | — | Monotone-phase mechanisms (Gram, Rosser, Turing) are counting devices that fail pointwise because S(t) is unbounded. The AFE symmetry gives Z real but not its zero count; WD would need exact control of S(t) at scale 1/log t, which is pointwise information again. |
| 174 | Amplitude–frequency monotonicity as the real-rootedness mechanism. In Ξ(t) = ∫Φ(u)cos(tu)du the variable u is a frequency, and in the RS main sum the frequencies log n carry amplitudes n^{−½}. Pólya's finite-interval theorem gives real zeros when amplitude INCREASES with frequency, and two-term sums a cos λt + b cos μt with λ > μ are real-rooted iff the high-frequency amplitude dominates (e.g. A + cos t has non-real zeros for A > 1: f′² − ff″ = 1 + A cos t). Conjecture: some weighted monotonicity of Φ in u (or of n^{−½} in log n) yields WD. | WRONG MONOTONICITY (paper). Both Φ(u) and n^{−½} DECREASE in frequency, the regime where finite truncations have non-real zeros (step kernels, row 139; Dirichlet partial sums, row 172; A + cos t). Real-rootedness of Ξ, if true, cannot come from monotonicity: it must come from the super-exponential decay of Φ acting on the whole line, a global property destroyed by truncation and damping (rows 139/154 [L corpus no-gos]). | — | Real-rootedness is not a monotonicity phenomenon here: amplitudes decrease with frequency, which favours non-real zeros locally. Only the global tail structure of Φ (theta modularity) can compensate, the same exact-identity requirement as rows 131/132. |
| 175 | Universal-factor (de Bruijn) contraction as the "global tail" mechanism of row 174 (searched first: corpus `SuperExpKernel.zeros_mul_cosh`, `zeros_mul_cosh_pow`, `riemannHeat_zeros_real`, `polyaKernel_*`). Multiplying the kernel by cosh(au) or by e^{λu²} contracts the zero strip. Conjecture: an iterated or optimized family of such universal factors, applied to Φ itself (factor ≡ 1 at the end), contracts the known strip \|Im z\| ≤ ½ to the real axis. | CIRCULAR (corpus [L]). de Bruijn's contraction is sharp: zeros in \|Im z\| ≤ Δ give H_λ zeros in \|Im z\| ≤ √(max(Δ² − 2λ, 0)), so reaching the real axis needs λ ≥ Δ²/2 (= 1/8 from Δ = ½, the corpus theorem `riemannHeat_zeros_real`). A factor that is ≡ 1 contracts nothing. Cosh factors are cosine shifts (row 155), whose real-rootedness is a zero-free-strip statement. Every universal factor moves the source (λ > 0 or a ≠ 0), and by no-slack (row 131 [L]) no source deformation can be pulled back to λ = 0. | — | The global-tail mechanism available (universal factors) is a contraction with exact cost Δ²/2 and cannot act at zero cost. Real-rootedness of Φ's transform itself needs Δ = 0 from the start: RH. |
| 176 | Lower the heat threshold Λ toward 0 (row 175: cost Δ²/2). Conjecture: a local version of the contraction (zeros at height ≥ T lie in \|Im\| ≤ Δ(T) = ½ − c(log T)^{−2/3}(log log T)^{−1/3}) plus a finite-height check gives H_λ real-rooted for some λ < 1/8, and iterating drives λ → 0. | FAILS TO REACH 0 (paper). The contraction cost is governed by sup_T Δ(T)²/2, and Δ(T) → ½ as T → ∞ (VK region shrinks toward Re s = 1), so the tail heights always demand λ ≥ (½ − o(1))²/2. Effective improvements (Ki–Kim–Lee Λ < ½ in the classical normalization; Polymath 15 Λ ≤ 0.22) use a barrier argument with a large finite-height numerical verification plus asymptotics. That is excluded here (no computations) and gives fixed positive bounds. Iteration has no mechanism: each step needs a smaller strip at ALL large heights, the zero-free-strip problem. | — | Λ upper bounds stall at the zero-free-strip width at infinity. Any Λ ≤ ε needs Δ(∞) ≤ √(2ε), a quasi-RH (rows 103/142/155 again). |
| 177 | Multiplicity law for direct-slice detection of mixed classes (generalizes row 111; searched first, no corpus analogue beyond row 44's detection at γ itself). For a critical zero of multiplicity m at the ordinate of γ ± iδ, the direct slice at t = γ + d is (1 + αv)^m(1 + bv + cv²) with α = d⁻², b = 2(d²−δ²)/R², c = 1/R², R = d² + δ². Conjecture: the v^{m+1} coefficient is α^{m−1}((m+2)d² − 2δ²)/(d²R²), so detection holds exactly on (m+2)d² < 2δ². | PROVED [L] (`RHTower/XiDirectMixedMultiplicityLaw.lean`: `coeff_one_add_C_mul_X_pow`, `coeff_directMixedMultiple`, `coeff_directMixedMultiple_neg_iff`; standard axioms; wired). Detection interval 0 < \|t − γ\| < δ√(2/(m+2)): m = 1 gives √(2/3) (row 111), m = 2 gives 1/√2, and the interval shrinks like δ√(2/m) without closing. (Paper side-remark: c_m turns negative at d² = δ²/(m−1) once m ≥ 8, inside the same interval.) | `RHTower/XiDirectMixedMultiplicityLaw.lean` | Mixed classes stay visible to the direct slice for every companion multiplicity, and invisible to reciprocal coefficients for every m ≥ 1. The direct slice is the robust detector; high multiplicity only narrows its window. |
| 178 | Exact direct bad set of the multiplicity-m mixed configuration (refines row 177 [L], which uses only c_{m+1}). With u = δ²/d², c_j ∝ C(m,j)(1+u)² + 2(1−u)C(m,j−1) + C(m,j−2). Conjecture: for large m the bad set is still governed by c_{m+1} (window δ√(2/(m+2))). | FALSE for large m (paper, asymptotic). For j = m − r the quadratic in u has discriminant/C(m,j)² ≈ m²/((r+1)²(r+2)) − 4m/(r+1), positive once m > 4(r+1)(r+2). Its negative u-interval has lower end u₋ ≈ (m/(r+1))(1 − 1/√(r+2)), so c_{m−r} is negative for d²/δ² up to ≈ (r+1)/(m(1 − 1/√(r+2))). Optimizing r ≈ √m/2 gives a bad window \|t − γ\| ≍ δ·m^{−1/4}, wider than the c_{m+1} window δ√(2/m). Exact small-m check: m ≤ 8 is governed by c_{m+1} (m = 8 touches at u = 5); m ≥ 9 is enlarged by c_m. Not formalized (asymptotic constants). | — | High-multiplicity companions narrow the direct detection window only like m^{−1/4}: detection is more robust than row 177's lower bound suggests. Model-level; no source-side content. |
| 179 | Where is the no-slack principle (row 131 [L]) realized locally? Conjecture: under RH the WD margin against backward heat flow at height t is controlled by the smallest local gap g(t) between consecutive critical zeros (a Lehmer-pair margin λ_crit(t) ≍ −g(t)²), so Λ = 0 together with RH forces arbitrarily small normalized gaps. | CONSISTENT AND CLASSICAL (paper; Csordas–Smith–Varga Lehmer-pair bounds; Newman–Rodgers–Tao [L corpus]). Under backward heat flow, two zeros at distance g approach with speed ≍ 1/g and collide after heat time ≍ g², then leave the line as a complex pair. Λ ≥ 0 ([L]) with Λ ≤ 0 (RH) needs collisions at arbitrarily small negative times, i.e. arbitrarily small normalized gaps (Lehmer pairs), the input of the CSV lower bounds and of Rodgers–Tao's dynamics. It is a consequence of RH + Newman, not a route to RH. | — | The rigid boundary at λ = 0 is realized by Lehmer pairs: WD holds with zero margin in the heat parameter exactly because critical zeros come arbitrarily close. Any WD producer must therefore be insensitive to small gaps, i.e. not a quantitative margin argument (row 130). |
| 180 | Milestone (rows 151–179). Lean: `RiemannKernelMomentsNotMultiplier` (multiplier sequences are Turán/log-concave via corpus Newton, rigid at γ → 1, Riemann's moments strictly log-convex, hence not a multiplier sequence; closes Pólya–Schur together with corpus CANNOT-497) and `XiDirectMixedMultiplicityLaw` (direct detection window δ√(2/(m+2)) for a multiplicity-m companion). Corrections: row 152 (corpus LP-symbol lane supersedes 126(b)). Paper closures: inward propagation (151), truncation placement (154), cosine shifts/HB family (155/156), threshold interpolation (157), function-field deformation (158), small values (159), positivity on ℝ (160), Gram families (161), descent (162), slice complementarity (163), margins and multiscale (164/165), complex-x geometry (166), Hardy moments (167), quantifier map (168/169), converse density (170), RS/Gram/amplitude/universal factors/Λ bounds (171–176), multiplicity asymptotics (178), Lehmer-pair realization of no-slack (179). | SUMMARY | — | Status unchanged since row 150. Detection side is complete at model level and extended (multiplicity, dichotomy, countermodels). Producer constraints (i)–(iv) of row 150 stand, now sharpened by the quantifier map: every unconditional input is ∃, μ (with positive error) or ∀-edge, and WD is ∀-centre and rigid. |
| 181 | ∀-centre Euler-using statements that would suffice: convergence of partial Euler products on the critical line. Ramanujan/Conrad-type results: if Π_{p≤x}(1 − p^{−s})^{−1} converges (suitably renormalized) at points of Re s = ½, then RH holds, with the √2 factor ζ(s)√2 appearing in the limit. Conjecture: such convergence can be proved directly from prime-sum estimates, giving an Euler-using ∀-centre input for WD. | OVERSHOOTS (paper; classical). Convergence of Σ_{p≤x} p^{−½−it} (the log of the partial product, up to absolutely convergent terms) at every t requires cancellation in prime sums at the square-root scale uniformly in x. That is stronger than RH: it implies RH and a strong uniform form of the prime number theorem error, ψ(x) = x + o(√x log²x)-type with uniform control in t. Mean-square convergence holds (Σ_{p≤x}\|p^{−½−it}\|² ~ log log x diverges slowly, so even L² convergence fails without renormalization). No unconditional route exists. | — | The ∀-centre Euler-using statements known to imply RH (Euler product convergence on the line, one-signed multiplicative sums, row 149) are strictly stronger than RH and unproven or false. The empty cell of row 83 contains no known statement weaker than or equal to RH that is provable. |
| 182 | Sieve positivity. Selberg's Λ² device produces nonnegative quadratic forms (Σ_{d\|n}λ_d)² ≥ 0 and hence majorants. Conjecture: a Λ²-type quadratic construction in the Dirichlet coefficients of ζ near s = ½ + it gives a lower bound c_{2j}(t) ≥ 0, i.e. product positivity from sieve squares (cf. the mollifier quadratic forms of rows 84/104). | PARITY BARRIER (paper; classical). Λ² squares give upper bounds (majorants). Lower bounds for sign-sensitive quantities require breaking parity: Selberg's parity problem shows sieve weights cannot distinguish n with λ(n) = +1 from λ(n) = −1. WD positivity at a fixed height detects off-line zeros, whose existence is equivalent to large signed oscillation of Σλ(n)n^{−½−it}-type sums, a parity-sensitive quantity. Bombieri's asymptotic sieve makes the parity barrier quantitative at level of distribution θ ≤ 1, the same threshold as the bandwidth wall (θ > 1 needed, rows 84/104). | — | Sieve squares are majorant devices and fail for WD's minorant problem; the parity barrier and the bandwidth wall are the same θ = 1 threshold seen from the sieve and from the mean-value side. |
| 183 | Break parity (row 182) with bilinear/Type II input (corpus Iwaniec 10/49 reconstruction, Maynard-block modules), or with Chowla-type sign cancellation for λ (Tao and Tao–Teräväinen logarithmically averaged Chowla/Elliott). Conjecture: a parity-breaking estimate controls the signed sums governing WD at each height. | AVERAGED / WRONG TARGET (paper; corpus memories iwaniec-level-of-distribution-law, deligne-amplification-obstruction). Parity-breaking theorems (Friedlander–Iwaniec, Heath-Brown) give asymptotics for primes in special thin sets via algebraic structure, and Chowla/Elliott results are logarithmic averages over n (and, in the Matomäki–Radziwiłł theory, over short intervals almost everywhere). None gives sign control of Σλ(n)n^{−½−it} at a fixed t; they are μ-type (row 168). Bilinear estimates at level θ < 1 stay inside the bandwidth wall. | — | Known parity-breaking is either structural (thin sets) or averaged (Chowla/MR). Neither reaches the ∀-centre, fixed-height sign information WD needs. |
| 184 | Local purity → global WD? For L(s, Δ) the Ramanujan–Petersson bound \|τ(p)\| ≤ 2p^{11/2} is a proven ∀-statement (Deligne, via Weil's RH for varieties): every Satake parameter lies on the unit circle. Conjecture: local purity of all Euler factors, together with the FE, forces WD for the completed L-function (for ζ the analogous local purity is trivial: all Satake parameters equal 1). | FALSE as an implication (paper). GRH for L(s, Δ) remains open although Ramanujan is a theorem, so local purity + FE ⇏ global zero location. For ζ local purity is trivial and carries no information. The Davenport–Heilbronn mechanism (linear combinations of Euler products with pure local data) shows that purity of the coefficients is compatible with off-line zeros once the Euler product is lost, and with the Euler product, purity is exactly the Selberg-class Ramanujan axiom (row 127), already insufficient by itself. | — | Local purity (Weil/Deligne at each p) does not globalize: the global WD/RH statement needs a positivity linking ALL primes at once (Weil positivity / Frobenius on H¹ in the function-field case, rows 88/134), not purity prime by prime. |
| 185 | WD inside the Weil framework (row 184 asks for positivity linking all primes, i.e. Weil positivity). Each power sum p_k(t) = Σ_ρ(t−γ_ρ)^{−2k} is a linear (Weil-type) functional of the zero measure, with test function τ^{−2k} in finite-part form (row 79). The WD coefficients are c_{2j}/c₀ = e_j(w) = [y^j]exp(Σ_k(−1)^{k+1}p_k y^k/k). Conjecture: WD at every height is the Weil positivity cone restricted to a family {h_{t,j}}, so the corpus Weil-cone lanes apply directly. | NOT A CONE CONDITION (paper; rows 69/96). e_j is a degree-j polynomial in the linear functionals p_k, so WD is an exponential (nonlinear) positivity of a Weil-linear generating function, not membership in the linear Weil cone. The sufficient linear condition, (−1)^{k+1}p_k ≥ 0 for all k (exp of a nonnegative series), is false at heights whose nearest zero is off-line or shadowed (row 96(b)). Conversely Weil-cone positivity (linear in the zeros) implies RH and hence WD, but only through the full cone (row 90), not through any finite family of h_{t,j}. | — | WD and Weil positivity meet only at RH: WD is exp-of-linear (product-type) positivity, Weil is linear positivity, and no finite linear family certifies the exponential positivity at a given height. |
| 186 | WD order j as a j-level correlation functional (refines row 185). c_{2j}/c₀ = e_j(w) = Σ_{ρ₁<…<ρ_j} Π_i (t−γ_{ρ_i})^{−2} is a sum over j-tuples of distinct zeros with a product test function. Height-averaged against a weight, it becomes a j-level correlation sum Σ_{distinct j-tuples} F(γ₁ − γ₂, …) with F the finite-part convolution of the product kernels. Conjecture: Rudnick–Sarnak j-level correlation theorems (restricted Fourier support) give averaged WD at every order, and their support extension gives pointwise WD. | BANDWIDTH WALL AGAIN (paper; rows 104/112). Rudnick–Sarnak/Montgomery compute j-level correlations only for test functions with Fourier support in a bounded region (Σ\|ξ_i\| < 2 type), while the WD kernels (t−γ)^{−2} have Fourier transforms \|ξ\|e^{−0·\|ξ\|} with unbounded support (singular at coincidences). Averaged WD at order j is therefore beyond known support, a j-level analogue of the \|α\| < 1 wall. Pointwise WD at fixed t is a single configuration statement, never an average. | — | Structural dictionary: WD order j ⟷ j-level correlation of zeros with singular product kernels at one point. Order 1 ⟷ density (one-level), order 2 ⟷ pair correlation, etc. All averaged versions sit behind the Fourier-support (bandwidth) wall, and the pointwise versions are configuration statements. |
| 187 | Configuration rigidity (row 186: pointwise WD is a configuration statement). Rigid point processes (Ghosh–Peres: Ginibre, sine/GUE process) have local configurations determined by the exterior: the exterior fixes the number of points inside a disc. Conjecture: the zeta zero set is "number-rigid" in this sense, using the explicit formula as the exterior-to-interior map, so off-line points inside a disc are excluded once the exterior is critical and the count matches N(T). | CIRCULAR / UNPROVEN (paper). Number rigidity of the zeta zeros is a statistical property of a model process (GUE/sine), not proven for ζ. Even granted, it fixes counts, not positions: an off-line pair can replace two critical points with the same count (counting is blind, rows 112/166). The explicit formula already gives exact count information (N(T) = main term + S(T)), and S(t) is controlled only to O(log t) (row 173), which does not fix counts in discs of radius ≍ 1/log t. | — | Rigidity of the zero process would control counts, not the line, and the actual control of counts (S(t)) is too weak at the zero spacing. Configuration statements need positional, not numerical, rigidity. |
| 188 | Positional rigidity from the Klein-four symmetry. Zeros come in orbits {ρ, ρ̄, 1−ρ, 1−ρ̄}: size 2 on the line, size 4 off it. Conjecture: an exact mod-4 count of zeros in symmetric regions (from N(T) and the argument principle), compared with a mod-4 count of critical zeros (sign changes of Z), forces the off-line orbit count to vanish. | REDISCOVERED / WALLED (corpus: `OrbitParityOffLine` (`not_odd_sum_of_offCritical`), metaplectic Kramers parity wall memory). Off-line orbits contribute 0 mod 4 to the total count and 0 mod 2 to the critical count, so parity and mod-4 invariants are blind to whole off-line quadruples: any off-line orbit can be added or removed without changing them. The corpus parity lane records exactly this wall (odd-order phenomena above the line are detectable; even-order quadruples are not). | — | Discrete symmetry yields parity invariants that see only odd defects. Off-line zeros come in even orbits by symmetry, so parity can never exclude them; positional rigidity cannot come from the Klein group. |
| 189 | Positional control through S(t) at the zero spacing (rows 173/187 need it). Selberg's exact formula: S(t) = −(1/π)Σ_{n≤x²}Λ_x(n)sin(t log n)/(√n log n) + (terms over zeros ρ with \|γ − t\| ≲ 1/log x, weighted by (β − ½)) + O(·). Conjecture: choosing x = t^{θ} with θ large, the prime part controls S(t) at scale 1/log t pointwise, fixing local positions and hence WD. | CIRCULAR (paper). The zero term in Selberg's formula is proportional to the off-line displacement Σ(β − ½)⁺ of the zeros near t, so it vanishes identically only under RH. Unconditionally it is bounded only on average (mean values of Σ(β − ½), rows 104/170). With θ > 1 the prime part needs the long-mollifier/bandwidth input; with θ ≤ 1 the formula determines S(t) only to O(1/θ)-type accuracy, i.e. O(1) zeros' uncertainty. | — | S(t) at the zero spacing is controlled exactly by the off-line displacement near t: Selberg's formula is the exact statement that positional control ⟺ zero-free-near-t, the WD pointwise problem again. |
| 190 | Determinantal structure (follow-up to row 186's j-level reading). If the zeros near height t form a determinantal process (GUE/sine kernel), the j-level correlation functions ρ_j = det[K(γ_a, γ_b)] are ≥ 0, and averaged WD at order j, ∫Π_i(t−γ_i)^{−2}ρ_j dγ, is a positive kernel against a positive density. Conjecture: determinantal positivity of the actual zero correlations gives averaged, and then pointwise, WD. | CIRCULAR (paper). The determinantal sine-kernel model is a model of REAL points (critical zeros); its positivity presupposes RH. Montgomery's pair correlation and Rudnick–Sarnak's j-level correlations are proven only for restricted Fourier support (row 186), and they concern ordinates, not real parts: they are computed assuming RH, or for the multiset of ordinates without positional information. Off-line zeros are invisible to ordinate correlations except through coincidences (row 112). | — | Random-matrix structure lives on the ordinates of a real point set; it presupposes the line. Determinantal positivity is a model property of the RH world, not a producer. |
| 191 | Mellin–Barnes representation of the slice coefficients. c_{2j}(t) = (1/(2j)!)∂_x^{2j}[ξ(½+x+it)ξ(½−x+it)]_{x=0} = Σ_{a+b=2j}(±)ξ^{(a)}ξ^{(b)}/(a!b!), and each ξ^{(a)}(½+it) has a Mellin–Barnes/contour representation through Γ and ζ shifted into the Euler region (∂^a of Γ(s/2)π^{−s/2}ζ(s) via Cauchy on a circle of radius > ½ reaching Re s > 1). Conjecture: moving every contour into the Euler region writes c_{2j}(t) as an absolutely convergent positive combination of Euler-product values. | FAILS (paper; row 103 mechanism). Cauchy circles of radius R > ½ around ½ + it give c_{2j}(t) = ∮ g_t(x)x^{−2j−1}dx/(2πi). The arc inside the strip contributes oscillatory values of ζ (convexity-size, signed), and the arc in the Euler region contributes Euler-product values with phases e^{−2ijθ}. Positivity needs the saddle in the Euler region, which happens exactly for j ≥ (¼+ε)log t (the quarter edge [L]). For j below the edge the saddle sits in the strip and the representation is signed. | — | Contour/Mellin–Barnes representations reproduce the quarter-edge dichotomy: positivity for orders whose saddle lies in the Euler region, no sign below it. Same as rows 103/142. |
| 192 | Averaged order-2 WD and Montgomery's F(α). By row 186, the height average of e₂(w) = Σ_{ρ<ρ′}(t−γ)^{−2}(t−γ′)^{−2} is a pair-correlation sum with a kernel whose Fourier transform grows like \|α\|. Conjecture: averaged order-2 WD ⟸ Montgomery's pair-correlation conjecture (F(α) = 1 for \|α\| ≥ 1), and F on [1, ∞) might be accessible through the WD structure. | CIRCULAR (paper). Montgomery's F(α) is defined for ordinates of critical zeros and computed for \|α\| < 1 ASSUMING RH. The conjecture F ≡ 1 on \|α\| ≥ 1 is a statement inside the RH world. Moreover, a kernel with \|α\|-growth needs F on all of ℝ, i.e. the full pair-correlation conjecture, which is beyond the bandwidth wall at \|α\| = 1 (rows 112/186). The WD structure supplies no information about F beyond \|α\| < 1. | — | Averaged order-j WD sits behind the j-level correlation conjectures, which themselves presuppose RH. No unconditional access to the \|α\| ≥ 1 regime exists through slices. |
| 193 | Absolute monotonicity form (corpus `XiHorizontalAbsoluteMonotonicityStrip`/`Window`). For entire g, nonnegative Taylor coefficients at 0 ⟺ g absolutely monotone on [0, ∞), so WD ⟺ x ↦ \|ξ(½+x+it)\|² is absolutely monotone on x ≥ 0 at every height. Conjecture: Bernstein-type structure for absolutely monotone functions (Laplace-transform representation g(x) = ∫e^{xs}dμ(s) with μ ≥ 0 on [0, ∞)) can be verified from the source. | REDISCOVERED / FAILS (corpus modules; rows 55/96/138). Bernstein's representation with μ ≥ 0 requires absolute monotonicity on (−∞, 0] as well, i.e. in the other direction. For g_t that means Ξ(t+u)Ξ(t−u) absolutely monotone in −u² ≤ 0, which fails (Ξ changes sign; rows 55/96(a)). On [0, ∞) alone, absolute monotonicity is exactly coefficient positivity, with no integral representation to verify. The corpus already owns the absolute-monotonicity strip and window formulations (unconditional at the Euler edge, rows 6a/142). | — | Absolute monotonicity is WD verbatim; its Bernstein representation would need two-sided monotonicity, which is false. No new handle. |
| 194 | Two-variable Taylor data at the centre. G(t, x) = Ξ(t − ix)Ξ(t + ix) is entire in (t, x), with Taylor coefficients a_{m,j} = ∂_t^m c_{2j}(0)/m! at (0, 0), which are computable from the moments of Φ (row 77). Conjecture: a sign/log-concavity pattern of the finitely-indexed array (a_{m,j}) at the centre (corpus centre results: Turán, PF₃₇, log-concave Taylor coefficients `xi_centre_logConcave`) forces c_{2j}(t) ≥ 0 for all real t. | FAILS (paper). c_{2j}(t) is even and oscillatory in t: a_{m,j} alternate in sign with m (cosine-transform structure, row 77: c_{2j} = M̂_j/(2j)!). Global nonnegativity of an entire function on ℝ is not determined by sign patterns of its Taylor coefficients at one point (e.g. 1 − t² + t⁴/2 > 0 vs 1 − t² + t⁴/4 with real zeros; same sign pattern). Centre data are the t = 0 instance of WD (corpus PF results), the low-height end of the problem, not a global certificate. | — | Centre (t = 0) data, however complete, are a single-height statement; global-in-t positivity is not encoded in local Taylor sign patterns. |
| 195 | Heat-time derivative at λ = 0. With H_λ the de Bruijn–Newman deformation (∂_λH_λ = ±∂_z²H_λ in the corpus normalization), the slice coefficients c_{2j}^{(λ)}(t) are entire in λ. Conjecture: under the known facts (WD for λ ≥ 1/8 [L], failure for every λ < 0 [L]), the λ-derivative ∂_λ c_{2j}^{(λ)}(t)\|_{λ=0} has a sign forcing c_{2j}^{(0)}(t) ≥ 0 (a "monotone in λ" principle restricted to the boundary). | FAILS (paper; rows 91/131). ∂_λ acts on the slice as the anti-heat/heat combination ½(∂_x² − ∂_t²) (row 53/91 dictionary), which mixes c_{2j+2} with t-derivatives of c_{2j} and has no sign. Failure for every λ < 0 is compatible with c^{(0)} ≥ 0 or not: it constrains how positivity is lost (through collisions of Lehmer pairs, row 179), not the value at λ = 0. Monotonicity in λ would be an open condition near λ = 0, excluded by no-slack (row 131 [L]). | — | No boundary-derivative principle: the λ-flow at 0 is signless, and positivity at λ = 0 is a rigid boundary fact not visible to first-order λ data. |
| 196 | Log-concavity of the source as the global-tail mechanism (rows 174/175 ask for global structure of Φ). Conjecture: positivity + evenness + super-exponential decay + (strict) log-concavity of the kernel imply WD at order 1 (the first Laguerre inequality, c₂ ≥ 0) at every height, and inductively higher orders. | CLOSED IN THE CORPUS (searched first). `HTGeometry/LaguerreKernelCounterexample`: the positive, even, log-concave kernel e^{−πu²}(cosh(ωu) + 6/5) has a Fourier transform violating the first Laguerre inequality, so c₂ < 0 at some height. `RHTower/ThetaSourceStrictLogConcavityFluxNoGo`: a positive, even, super-exponentially decaying, globally strictly log-concave cosine-modulated Gaussian whose Laplace transform has a zero in the open right half-plane ("any successful source theorem needs additional global frequency rigidity"). | — | Local shape properties of the source (positivity, decay, log-concavity) do not imply even order-1 WD; the corpus has explicit countermodels. The needed global frequency rigidity is the arithmetic (theta/Euler) structure, consistent with rows 131/146/148. |
| 197 | Correction to row 168 via recurrence (searched first: corpus `ZetaStrongRecurrenceReceiver`, `riemannHypothesis_of_zetaStronglyRecurrent` [L]). μ-type statements CAN imply ∀: Bagchi's strong recurrence (positive lower density of shifts τ with max_K\|ζ(s+iτ) − ζ(s)\| < ε on compacts K ⊂ {½ < σ < 1}) implies RH, since a zero ρ would recur near ρ + iτ for a positive density of τ (Rouché), contradicting the zero-density bound N(σ, T) = o(T). Conjecture: the slice analogue, positive density of heights τ with g_{t+τ} ≈ g_t on a neighbourhood of the bad scale, is provable from WD's density results and gives RH. | RECEIVER (corpus [L]) / CIRCULAR as producer. The mechanism is genuine: recurrence + zero density converts a single off-line zero into ≫ T of them. But strong recurrence is equivalent to RH (Bagchi); Voronin universality gives approximation only of NONVANISHING targets, and ζ itself is a nonvanishing target on K iff RH holds there. The WD density results (rows 104/129) say most heights are good, not that the slice at a given t recurs. | — | Refined quantifier map: μ-type statements imply ∀ when they are recurrence statements for a fixed configuration (Bagchi), not when they are density-of-good-heights statements. Recurrence of ζ to itself is RH-equivalent and owned by the corpus as a receiver. |
| 198 | Where the WD bad measure lives (follow-up to rows 104/197). Exposure windows of off-line zeros with δ ≥ δ₀ have total length ≤ 2Σ_{δ≥δ₀}δ ≤ N(½ + δ₀; T, 2T) ≪ T^{1−cδ₀}log T = o(T) (zero density). Conjecture: the WD bad density is carried entirely by near-line zeros, δ ≲ C/log T, so a theorem excluding only near-line off-line zeros would give WD at almost every height with error o(T), and with Bagchi-type recurrence (row 197) at every height. | TRUE (first part) / CIRCULAR (second) (paper). Selberg's N(½ + δ, T) ≪ T^{1−δ/4}log T makes the far contribution o(T) for every fixed δ₀ > 0, so the positive constant ε(θ) of row 104 comes from zeros with δ ≲ 1/log T (the off-line moment Σ(β−½)⁺ ≍ T is dominated by them). But excluding near-line off-line zeros is the hardest part: they are the shadowed, near-double configurations (rows 24/41/95) invisible to every averaged method, and recurrence of ζ is RH-equivalent (row 197). | — | Sharpened localization: WD's density defect is a near-line phenomenon (δ ≍ 1/log T). Far off-line zeros cost o(T) in bad measure by zero density. The open core is exactly near-line exclusion, the pointwise problem at the zero-spacing scale. |
| 199 | Near-line exclusion via ξ′ (row 198's open core). A near-line off-line pair ½ ± δ + iγ (δ ≪ 1/log γ) behaves like a perturbed double critical zero. By the symmetry ξ′(s) = −ξ′(1 − s), ξ′ has a zero on the critical line at height ≈ γ between the pair. Conjecture: counting critical zeros of ξ′ (Conrey: ≥ 79% of zeros of ξ′ on the line; Levinson–Montgomery relations) detects or excludes near-line pairs. | NO CONTRADICTION (paper). Near-line pairs ADD critical zeros of ξ′ (one between each pair) instead of removing them, so lower bounds for critical zeros of ξ′ are consistent with them. Speiser/Levinson–Montgomery link zeros of ζ′ LEFT of the line to off-line zeros of ζ only on average (row 144), and a near-line pair need not create a left zero of ζ′. Counting zeros of derivatives is blind to near-line pairs at the proportion level. | — | Near-line off-line pairs are camouflaged as double critical zeros at every level available: counting of ξ and ξ′ zeros, slices (near-double law, row 24), collision charge. Their exclusion needs positional resolution below the zero spacing. |
| 200 | Milestone and unification (rows 181–199). Resolving zero positions below the zero spacing 2π/log T from prime data obeys an uncertainty principle: prime sums of length X resolve ordinates to ≍ 2π/log X (Nyquist), so resolution δ ≪ 1/log T needs log X ≫ log T, i.e. θ = log X/log T > 1 (corpus memory bandwidth-one-nyquist-wall). Claim: every open core met in rows 36–199 is this one statement: the long-mollifier problem (θ > 1, rows 84/104), the parity barrier at θ = 1 (182), the \|α\| < 1 support wall for j-level correlations (112/186/192), S(t) control at the spacing (173/189), near-line exclusion (198/199), positional rigidity (187/188). | CONSOLIDATED (paper; corpus wall). All are instances of the θ = 1 bandwidth wall: unconditional arithmetic input (primes up to T^θ, θ ≤ 1, or Fourier support < 1) cannot resolve the zero configuration below its own spacing, while WD's open core (near-line pairs, δ ≲ 1/log T) lives exactly there. The no-slack principle (row 131 [L]) and the quantifier map (rows 168/169/197) are the analytic and logical shadows of the same wall. Lean added since row 180: none beyond rows 153/177. | SUMMARY | — | Route WD's state, unified: detection complete [L] at model level; producer = a statement resolving the zero configuration below the zero spacing using arithmetic (Euler/ℤ-specific) input with zero slack, equivalently crossing the θ = 1 bandwidth wall. No known method crosses it. |
| 201 | Partial crossings of the θ = 1 wall (row 200). Known extensions of mollifier/twisted-moment length use Kloosterman-sum technology (Deshouillers–Iwaniec): Conrey's θ = 4/7 for the mollified second moment, Bettin–Chandee–Radziwiłł's θ = ½ + δ for twisted second moments with general coefficients; distributional crossings (BFI, Zhang/Maynard) go beyond ½ only for special (well-factorable/smooth) weights. Conjecture: an extension of these techniques reaches θ > 1 for WD's quadratic forms. | OUT OF REACH (paper; corpus memories estermann-kloosterman-q-aspect, deligne-amplification-obstruction). Every known crossing stays below θ = 1 and uses Weil/Kloosterman bounds that save √q, which are ζ-blind (corpus Estermann/Kloosterman memory). Pushing past θ = 1 for the second moment would already give a positive proportion of critical zeros approaching 100% (rows 104/121) and is the long-standing mollifier barrier. No known mechanism crosses θ = 1. | — | The best partial crossings (θ = 4/7, ½ + δ) are far from θ = 1, and the Kloosterman savings that power them are ζ-blind. WD inherits the full gap between 4/7 and > 1. |
| 202 | ζ-specific (non-Kloosterman) input to cross θ = 1 (row 201: Kloosterman savings are ζ-blind). Candidates: the Estermann function D(s, h/k) = Σ d(n)e(nh/k)n^{−s} with its exact functional equation (corpus Estermann zeta [L], Estermann analytic/progression lanes), the exact additive structure of d(n), and the twist-independent main term. Conjecture: Estermann's FE (ζ²-specific) controls the off-diagonal of the twisted second moment beyond θ = 1. | FAILS (corpus memories estermann-zeta-in-lean, estermann-analytic-layer, estermann-progression-lane, estermann-kloosterman-q-aspect). The Estermann FE converts the off-diagonal into Kloosterman-type sums in the dual variable. Its main term is twist-independent (corpus), and the error terms are bounded by Weil's √q saving, which is ζ-blind and stops at θ = 4/7-type ranges. The FE is exact but symmetric (row 132(2)) and gives no positivity or extra cancellation beyond Weil. | — | ζ-specific exact identities (Estermann FE) reshuffle the off-diagonal but do not add cancellation beyond Weil/Kloosterman; the θ = 1 wall is not crossed by ζ² structure either. |
| 203 | Spectral input to cross θ = 1: Kuznetsov's formula and the Deshouillers–Iwaniec spectral large sieve power the mollifier extensions of row 201, and their ranges depend on the exceptional-eigenvalue parameter (Selberg's λ₁ ≥ 1/4; corpus Selberg 3/16 [L], Sarnak–Xue λ₁(Γ(p)) ≥ 5/36 − η [L], Selberg arithmetic routes capped memory). Conjecture: the full Selberg eigenvalue conjecture (or the corpus spectral bounds) pushes the mollifier length past θ = 1. | FAILS (paper; corpus memories selberg-three-sixteenths-lean, sarnak-xue-gap-lean, selberg-arithmetic-routes-capped). The exceptional-eigenvalue parameter enters the Kloosterman-sum bounds as a secondary loss (factors like X^{θ_spec} with θ_spec ≤ 7/64 known). Removing it entirely (Selberg's conjecture) improves exponents in the DI ranges modestly but leaves the main barrier, the length of the dual sum after Kuznetsov, at θ ≈ 4/7. The corpus records the arithmetic routes to Selberg's conjecture as capped at the 3/16 theta barrier. | — | Spectral improvements are secondary to the θ = 1 wall: even Selberg's eigenvalue conjecture would not move mollifier lengths past ≈ 4/7. Spectral and arithmetic walls stack; neither lifts the other. |
| 204 | Multiplicative chaos and low moments. Harper's "better than square-root cancellation" for random multiplicative functions and Heap–Radziwiłł–Soundararajan's sharp low moments of ζ(½+it) (∫\|ζ\|^{2k} ≍ T(log T)^{k²} for 0 < k < 1) describe ζ near the line through critical multiplicative chaos. Conjecture: chaos-type estimates control the slice at every height, beyond θ = 1, because they capture the full Euler product rather than a mollifier of length T^θ. | μ-TYPE (paper). Low-moment and chaos results are distributional (moments, distributions of log\|ζ\|), the μ-column of row 168. They capture the Euler product's statistics through random models valid on average over t; at a fixed t no chaos estimate applies. The "full Euler product" enters only through moments, which are averages. Their proofs use mollifier-type truncations of the Euler product at length T^{θ}, θ ≤ 1, plus Gaussian approximation, so they do not cross the wall either. | — | Multiplicative-chaos technology is the sharpest μ-type description of ζ near the line, still averaged, still behind θ = 1 for its arithmetic input. No ∀-centre information. |
| 205 | Fourier quasicrystals. The explicit formula says the zero "measure" Σ_ρ δ_{γ_ρ} has distributional Fourier transform supported on {±log n} plus an archimedean density, a Poisson-type summation (Dyson's quasicrystal remark). If all γ are real it is a positive measure on ℝ with discrete Fourier support. Classification theorems (Kurasov–Sarnak crystalline measures from stable polynomials; Olevskii–Ulanovskii: positive Fourier quasicrystals with uniformly discrete support and spectrum come from real-rooted exponential polynomials; Lev–Olevskii periodicity; corpus Meyer/Delone ℚ(√2) [L]). Conjecture: these classifications, applied to the zeta zero measure, force the zeros onto ℝ. | INAPPLICABLE / CIRCULAR (paper). (a) The classifications assume a positive measure on ℝ, i.e. real zeros, the conclusion. Off-line zeros make the "measure" a distribution on ℂ, outside the theory. (b) Their hypotheses (uniform discreteness or bounded density of support and spectrum) fail on both sides: the zeros have density (1/2π)log T → ∞ and {log n} accumulates.
