(c) The archimedean density term is not discrete. | — | Quasicrystal rigidity presupposes real support and bounded densities; the zeta zero set violates the density hypotheses on both sides, and realness is the conclusion. No route. |
| 206 | Local quasicrystal (repair of row 205's density obstruction). Unfold the zeros near height T to unit density, γ̃ = γ·(log T)/2π. In a window [T, T + H] the local explicit formula pairs them with prime powers through test functions whose Fourier transforms are supported in \|ξ\| ≤ 2π·θ (θ = support in units of log T). Conjecture: a local quasicrystal rigidity theorem (bounded densities now hold locally) applies window by window and forces local realness. | BANDWIDTH WALL (paper; rows 112/186/200). Locally the dual side is the set of prime powers up to T^{θ}, accessible only for θ ≤ 1 with known estimates, i.e. test functions with Fourier support < 1 in unfolded units. Quasicrystal rigidity needs the full (unbounded) spectrum, the complete explicit formula with all prime powers, beyond θ = 1. Truncating the spectrum at θ ≤ 1 leaves a measure determined only up to features below the zero spacing, exactly the near-line ambiguity (rows 198/199). Realness of the support is still assumed by the rigidity theorems. | — | Localization repairs the densities but truncates the spectrum at the bandwidth θ = 1, where rigidity is lost. Same wall as rows 112/186/200. |
| 207 | Pointwise ergodic theorems along primes (Bourgain; Wierdl) as a route to fixed-height statements. They give almost-everywhere convergence of averages (1/π(N))Σ_{p≤N} f(T^p x), i.e. statements for a.e. point of a dynamical system. Conjecture: realizing the slice family as an orbit of a measure-preserving flow (the scaling flow on the adele class space, or the t-shift on ζ-values à la Bohr/Bagchi) turns prime-averaged ergodic theorems into WD at every height. | μ-TYPE (paper). Pointwise ergodic theorems hold almost everywhere in the phase space, the μ-column again (row 168). Moreover the t-shift on ζ(s + it) is the Bohr/Bagchi setting, where universality and recurrence are a.e. or positive-density statements, and the everywhere version is RH-equivalent strong recurrence (row 197). The adelic scaling flow is the CCM setting, whose positivity is that lane's open endpoint (row 136). | — | Ergodic-theoretic statements are a.e. in phase space. Converting a.e. to everywhere needs openness plus nullity (row 169) or recurrence of a fixed point (row 197), both RH-strength here. |
| 208 | Formalize row 178's exact refinement at small multiplicity. | PROVED [L] (`XiDirectMixedMultiplicityLaw`: `coeff_directMixedMultiple_diag`, the v^m coefficient α^m + mα^{m−1}b + C(m,2)α^{m−2}c; `directMixedMultiple_nine_enlarged`: for m = 9 at 21d² = 4δ² the v⁹ coefficient equals −(23/625)d^{−18} < 0 while the v¹⁰ criterion fails, 11d² ≥ 2δ²). Standard axioms. | `RHTower/XiDirectMixedMultiplicityLaw.lean` | The exact direct bad set of a multiplicity-m mixed class is strictly larger than the c_{m+1} window for m = 9 (and, by row 178, asymptotically ≍ δm^{−1/4}). Model-level detection refinement. |
| 209 | Selberg-zeta benchmark (complements the function-field benchmark of row 88). For a compact or congruence surface, the Selberg zeta function Z_Γ has zeros ½ ± ir_j from Laplace eigenvalues λ_j = ¼ + r_j², which lie on the line exactly when λ_j ≥ ¼, plus real zeros s_j ∈ (½, 1) from exceptional eigenvalues λ_j < ¼. Conjecture: the WD slice for Z_Γ is positive at all heights iff λ₁ ≥ ¼, and the mechanism transfers to ζ. | TRUE FOR Z_Γ, NO TRANSFER (paper; corpus Selberg 3/16 [L], Sarnak–Xue [L], selberg-arithmetic-routes-capped). For Z_Γ the zeros are spectral (self-adjoint Laplacian), so WD at height t fails exactly through exceptional real zeros, at heights near 0, and holds otherwise: WD ⟺ no exceptional eigenvalues ⟺ Selberg's conjecture for congruence Γ (open; corpus 3/16). The mechanism is self-adjointness of a known operator. ζ has no known self-adjoint realization (row 100, spectral receivers). | — | Second benchmark: WD holds for Selberg zeta functions exactly by spectral self-adjointness, failing only through exceptional eigenvalues. For ζ the missing ingredient is again the operator (Hilbert–Pólya), outside WD. |
| 210 | Can WD construct the missing operator (rows 88/100/134/209)? In both benchmarks WD holds because an operator exists: Frobenius on H¹ with a Hodge/Castelnuovo positivity (function fields), the self-adjoint Laplacian (Selberg). Conjecture: the slice family itself defines an operator, e.g. multiplication/shift operators on the de Branges space ℋ(E) with E = Ξ′ − iΞ (row 99), or on Sonin/co-Poisson spaces (corpus Burnol lanes), whose self-adjointness is provable and whose determinant is Ξ. | CIRCULAR (paper; corpus Burnol/HF/CCM lanes). The de Branges space ℋ(E) has a canonical self-adjoint operator (multiplication by z with a boundary condition) whose spectrum is the zero set of a real combination of A, B; for it to be Ξ's zeros, E must be Hermite–Biehler, i.e. RH (row 99, `StrictHermiteBiehlerGivesRH`). Burnol's co-Poisson/Sonin spaces realize ζ-related operators but their spectral identification with the zeros, plus self-adjointness, is that lane's open endpoint. Determinant identities with Ξ for non-self-adjoint operators exist trivially (diagonal on the zeros) and carry no positivity. | — | WD cannot manufacture the operator: every operator naturally attached to the slice family is self-adjoint iff RH. The benchmarks show what is missing (a geometric/spectral object), and the corpus lanes that seek it (CCM, Deninger/HF, Burnol) have it as their open endpoints. |
| 211 | Herglotz/Pick form. For an LP function f, −f′/f is a Pick function (Im(−f′(z)/f(z)) > 0 for Im z > 0), and order-1 WD on ℝ is the real-axis boundary trace of this, (−f′/f)′ = c₂/c₀ ≥ 0. Conjecture: the Pick property of −Ξ′/Ξ in the upper half-plane can be established from Nevanlinna-class/boundary data (Ξ real on ℝ, growth), giving RH and all WD orders. | RECEIVER / REDISCOVERED (corpus weil-cone-herglotz-bridge memory, `XiHermiteBiehlerCount`). Pick property of −Ξ′/Ξ ⟺ no zeros of Ξ in the upper half-plane ⟺ RH. Boundary data on ℝ (Ξ real, c₂/c₀) do not determine the interior sign: the Herglotz representation needs positivity of the boundary measure, which is the zero distribution itself, and an off-line zero contributes a pole in Im z > 0 invisible to real-axis traces of bounded order (finite-order insufficiency). | — | Pick/Herglotz is the half-plane form of real-rootedness; boundary-order data (WD at bounded order) cannot certify interior pole-freeness. Same receiver as rows 48/99. |
| 212 | Revisit the frontier-map WD "Needs" item "global one-sided cap family with uniform integrability" through the corpus Ward-cap modules (searched: `XiWardSchwarzCapRealCharge`, `XiWardSchwarzCapKernel`, `ae_forall_xiWardSchwarzCapFactorIntegral_eq_two_re_half`). Conjecture: the canonical Ward cap's real signed charge (conjugate-even under height reversal, a.e. equal to 2Re(½)) can be bounded uniformly in the cap parameter from unconditional input, which would give the cap-family uniform integrability. | REDUCES TO THE SAME CORE (corpus docstring: "It supplies no size estimate: the real local mass at the moving Cauchy resonance is precisely the remaining analytic datum."). The remaining datum is the local zero mass near a moving resonance point on the line, a pointwise local-count statement at the zero-spacing scale (rows 165/189/198/200). Unconditional bounds give it only on average (μ) or with O(log t) fluctuations (S(t)), not uniformly. Also re-checked: positivity outside exposure windows is already corpus (`xiSliceCoeff_nonneg_of_not_mem_xiShadow`). | — | The Ward-cap formulation of WD's need is the local-mass problem at the moving resonance, the θ = 1 wall in Ward language. All three frontier-map WD "Needs" (cap integrability, low-order signs at every height, mixed-class loss) are now traced to the same core. |
| 213 | Length doubling through higher moments (crossing θ = 1 indirectly). ζ² has Dirichlet length ≍ T at the critical scale, and the fourth moment ∫\|ζ(½+it)\|⁴ (Ingham; Heath-Brown's asymptotic) controls length-T² objects. Conjecture: mollifying ζ² instead of ζ, i.e. mean values of \|ζ²M\|² with M of length T^θ, effectively doubles the usable length and crosses the θ = 1 wall for WD's quadratic forms. | FAILS (paper; classical ranges). The twisted fourth moment is known only for very short twists (Hughes–Young: θ < 1/11; Bettin–Bui–Li–Radziwiłł small improvements), far shorter than the second-moment ranges (4/7). Mollifying ζ² squares the off-diagonal complexity. Moreover ζ² mollification controls zeros of ζ² (same zeros, doubled multiplicity), so it gives no extra resolution below the zero spacing. Length in the Dirichlet-polynomial sense is not resolution in the zero sense: resolution needs primes beyond T, not products of short polynomials. | — | Higher moments do not convert into resolution: they lengthen the polynomial but shorten the admissible twist, and the uncertainty principle of row 200 is about prime support, which products do not extend. |
| 214 | Refinement of row 200's unification: information versus positivity. The Riemann–Siegel formula determines ζ(½+it) from n ≤ √(t/2π) up to an error O(t^{−1/4}), so the information needed to locate zeros to precision ≪ 1/log t is present pointwise in short prime and integer data (this is how zeros are verified numerically). Conjecture: the θ = 1 wall is an artefact of averaging methods, and a pointwise argument on the RS sum avoids it. | CORRECT DIAGNOSIS, NO MECHANISM (paper; rows 84/171/172/173). The θ = 1 wall constrains mean-value (μ-type) methods: averaging over t needs mollifier/twist lengths beyond T to resolve below the spacing. Pointwise, the RS sum carries full information, but WD needs a SIGN for the √t-term oscillatory sum at every t (no negative resonance), and no positivity mechanism for such finite exponential sums is known. Truncations alone fail (row 172: Montgomery's off-line zeros of partial sums); the symmetric AFE structure gives reality, not sign (row 173). | — | Two faces of the core, now separated. (A) Averaged methods: information-limited (θ = 1 wall). (B) Pointwise methods: information-complete (RS) but sign-less, the missing ingredient being a positivity principle for the symmetric √t-term RS sum at every height, the "no negative resonance" problem of row 84. |
| 215 | Attempt the RS positivity principle (row 214) at order 1. With Z_N(t) = 2Σ_{n≤N}a_n cos φ_n(t), a_n = n^{−½}, φ_n = θ(t) − t log n, the frequencies φ_n′ = θ′(t) − log n ≈ ½log(t/2π) − log n are positive and decreasing for n ≤ N = ⌊√(t/2π)⌋. The two-term identity of row 172 extends to W = Z_N′² − Z_N Z_N″ = 2Σ_{m,n}a_m a_n[(φ_m′+φ_n′)²cos(φ_m − φ_n) + (φ_m′−φ_n′)²cos(φ_m + φ_n)]/2 + (φ″ terms). Grouping: W = \|A₁\|² + Re(Ā₀A₂) + Re(e^{2iθ}B) with A_k(t) = Σ a_n(φ_n′)^k n^{it}, plus a fast term B. Conjecture: \|A₁\|² dominates the cross terms at every t. | FAILS (paper). A₁ is itself a Dirichlet polynomial of length √t and has zeros and small values (row 172: partial sums have off-line zeros), while Ā₀A₂ and the fast term need not vanish there. So W < 0 is possible wherever A₁ is small and the cross terms are negative. Domination pointwise is a "no simultaneous resonance" statement for the vector (A₀, A₁, A₂) of length-√t polynomials, which is exactly the row-84 problem with explicit components. | — | Explicit form of the missing positivity: order-1 WD for the RS main sum ⟺ \|A₁(t)\|² ≥ −Re(Ā₀A₂) − Re(e^{2iθ}B) at every t, for three explicit length-√t Dirichlet polynomials. No principle forces this; their joint small values are uncontrolled pointwise. |
| 216 | Where can W = Z′² − ZZ″ (order-1 WD for Hardy's Z, up to Γ-factor corrections) be negative? At zeros of Z, W = Z′² ≥ 0. At critical points (Z′ = 0), W = −ZZ″, negative exactly at positive local minima or negative local maxima of Z. Conjecture: order-1 WD for Z ⟺ Z has no positive local minimum and no negative local maximum (exactly one extremum between consecutive zeros), and this "monotone envelope" property is provable from the RS structure. | CLASSICAL RESTATEMENT, NOT RH-EQUIVALENT, NOT PROVABLE (paper; corpus finite-order insufficiency, rows 108/121/144/164). The equivalence is elementary calculus (W < 0 forces a critical point with ZZ″ > 0 nearby by continuity), and it is the classical "no positive minima of Z" property implied by RH. Order 1 is not RH-equivalent: shadowed far off-line pairs pass it (row 164). Unconditionally it fails exactly where Z has a "missing pair of zeros" locally (Lehmer-type near-misses are positive minima close to 0), and excluding positive minima at every t is the pointwise near-line problem (off-line pairs very near the line produce positive local minima of Z between their ordinates). | — | Order-1 WD = the absence of positive minima of Z, a classical, pointwise, still-open property; it is the near-line exclusion problem at order 1. The RS decomposition of row 215 is its trigonometric form. |
| 217 | Count the positive local minima of Ξ (row 216). Each positive local minimum of Ξ between consecutive real zeros forces an extra pair of zeros of Ξ′ off the critical line, i.e. a non-real zero pair of ξ′ near ½ + it (by Rolle and interlacing failure). Conjecture: Conrey's ≥ 79.9% of zeros of ξ′ on the line, or the corpus ξ′ proportions, bound the number of positive minima by ≤ 0.1·N(T), and a refinement drives it to 0. | μ-TYPE CAP (paper). The count of positive minima up to T is ≤ ½#(non-real zeros of ξ′ up to T) ≤ (0.101 + o(1))N(T) by Conrey's proportion, a density statement. Driving it to 0 needs 100% of zeros of ξ′ on the line, which is beyond proportion methods (same mollifier wall, rows 104/121/201). Moreover positive minima are only the order-1 shadow: absence of positive minima does not give RH (row 216). | — | Positive minima of Ξ are counted by non-real zeros of ξ′, so their density is bounded by Conrey-type proportions and can't reach 0 without long mollifiers. Order-1 phenomena inherit the full wall. |
| 218 | Known positivity principles for exponential sums as candidates for row 214's missing RS positivity: (a) Fejér/Bochner positive-definiteness (Σ\|b_n\|²-type diagonal ≥ 0 after averaging); (b) Turán's power-sum method, max_{M<ν≤M+N}\|Σ_j b_j z_j^ν\| ≥ (N/(8e(M+N)))^N·\|Σ b_j\| for \|z_j\| ≥ 1, used for zero-density and zero-free-region theorems; (c) Halász–Montgomery large-value bounds. Conjecture: one of them, specialized to the RS sum (A₀, A₁, A₂) of row 215, forces W(t) ≥ 0 at every t. | WRONG QUANTIFIER (paper; row 168). (a) Positive-definiteness yields averaged positivity (μ), as in the Husimi lane (rows 50–55 [L]). (b) Turán's method gives EXISTENCE of a large power sum for some ν in a window (∃-type), never a sign at a prescribed t; it powers zero-free regions near σ = 1 (edge-type). (c) Large-value bounds are upper bounds on the frequency of large values (μ). None produces a ∀-t lower bound or sign for a fixed oscillatory sum. | — | All known positivity/lower-bound principles for exponential sums are ∃ (Turán), μ (Fejér, Bochner, large values) or edge-type. A ∀-t sign principle for RS-type sums would be a new kind of theorem, which is what WD needs. |
| 219 | Search for an existing ∀-t sign principle for Dirichlet-type sums Σn^{−σ}cos(t log n + c). The only known one: Re ζ(σ + it) > 0 for all t once σ ≥ σ₀ ≈ 1.1925 (van de Lune), sharp in the sense that Re ζ takes negative values for some t whenever σ < σ₀. Conjecture: a weighted/symmetrized version (with the θ-phase and both AFE sums) has a ∀-t sign principle down to σ = ½. | IMPOSSIBLE BELOW THE EDGE (paper; classical). For ½ < σ ≤ 1, Bohr–Courant: {ζ(σ + it) : t ∈ ℝ} is dense in ℂ, so no fixed linear functional of ζ-values has a sign for all t. Universality (Voronin) makes this robust under any fixed analytic transformation. At σ = ½, Hardy's Z changes sign infinitely often, and its WD transforms are nonlinear exactly so as to avoid sign changes of values. Sign principles for Dirichlet sums therefore exist only for σ > σ₀ > 1, even beyond the Euler edge. | — | ∀-t sign principles for values of ζ are confined to σ ≥ σ₀ ≈ 1.19 by Bohr density and universality. WD's positivity, if true, must be of a genuinely nonlinear kind (product/elementary-symmetric in zeros), never a sign of a linear functional of ζ-values. This further constrains the missing principle. |
| 220 | Nonlinear principle as jet-cone invariance (row 219 forces nonlinearity). WD at order j is Q_j(J_j(t)) ≥ 0 for the indefinite Laguerre quadratic form Q_j on the jet J_j(t) = (Ξ(t), …, Ξ^{(2j)}(t)) ∈ ℝ^{2j+1}. Conjecture: the jet curve t ↦ J_j(t) is confined to the cone {Q_j ≥ 0} by a differential mechanism (an algebraic ODE satisfied by Ξ whose flow preserves the cone: a Lyapunov/invariant-region argument). | NO DIFFERENTIAL MECHANISM (paper; corpus HT lanes, Hölder/Hilbert–Ostrowski hypertranscendence). ζ, and hence Ξ, satisfies no algebraic differential equation (differential transcendence, corpus hypertranscendence layers). The jet curve is therefore not an orbit of any algebraic vector field, so cone invariance cannot come from an algebraic ODE flow. Transcendental ODEs exist trivially (Ξ solves its own linear ODE of infinite order) and carry no positivity. Any invariance must come from non-differential structure: FE (reflection), Euler product (multiplicative), theta modularity. | — | Structural link to the corpus HT program: hypertranscendence of ζ excludes invariant-region/Lyapunov proofs of WD at every order. The missing principle must be functional-equation or arithmetic, not differential, consistent with rows 131/132/146. |
| 221 | Non-differential cone invariance (row 220's remaining options). (a) FE/theta modularity acts on the jet curve as the reflection J(−t) = diag(±1)J(t) (Ξ even), a symmetry of the curve; the cone {Q_j ≥ 0} is reflection-invariant, so the FE is compatible with but does not force confinement. (b) The Euler product acts on ζ only in Re s > 1, i.e. on jets of the shifted slice at x₀ > ½ (row 151), where confinement holds unconditionally (row 6a) but does not pull back inward. (c) Modularity of θ at other cusps / Hecke relations reduce to (a) and (b) (rows 135/148). Conjecture: some combination confines J_j(t) at x = 0. | NO (paper). Each structure either is a symmetry (cannot force a sign), or acts at the Euler edge (outward-only, row 151), or reduces to these. A symmetry group acting on the curve preserves the cone but cannot exclude excursions out of it: excursions occur in reflection-symmetric pairs (±t), consistent with off-line quadruples (row 188). | — | The jet-curve picture closes the structural search: confinement of the WD jet curve needs a constraint beyond symmetry (FE) and edge positivity (Euler); hypertranscendence excludes differential constraints. The missing constraint is a new arithmetic identity at σ = ½ of non-symmetric, non-edge type. |
| 222 | Exact identities AT σ = ½ (row 221 asks for one): Siegel's exact Riemann–Siegel integral formula ζ(s) = ∫_{0↙1}x^{−s}e^{iπx²}(e^{iπx} − e^{−iπx})^{−1}dx + χ(s)·conj-type integral, and the theta self-duality θ(i) = π^{1/4}/Γ(¾) at the fixed point of z ↦ −1/z (the t = 0 centre of WD). Conjecture: the Siegel integral at s = ½ + x + it, with contour deformations through the saddle, gives a representation of the slice with manifestly nonnegative x-coefficients. | FAILS (paper). Siegel's formula is exact but its integrands are complex oscillatory (e^{iπx²} chirps), and positivity after saddle deformation gives exactly the RS asymptotics with signed remainder (rows 171/214): the same √t-term oscillatory structure. The theta fixed point gives positivity only at t = 0 (corpus centre results: Turán, PF₃₇), the low-height end. Other heights correspond to no fixed point. | — | The exact identities at σ = ½ (Siegel integral, theta self-duality) are oscillatory except at the self-dual centre, where WD is already known. Positivity at t ≠ 0 has no fixed-point mechanism. |
| 223 | Arithmetic square formulas (Waldspurger/Kohnen–Zagier type). For self-dual families, central values are squares of arithmetic coefficients, L(½, f ⊗ χ_D) = c_f·\|a(\|D\|)\|²/\|D\|^{k−½} ≥ 0: an exact, product-type, ℤ-specific positivity AT σ = ½, precisely the type rows 132/221 ask for. Conjecture: a square formula c_{2j}(t) = Σ_S\|F_S(t)\|² with arithmetically defined F_S (period integrals) exists at every height, giving WD. | ONLY AT SELF-DUAL POINTS (paper). Waldspurger-type formulas come from the theta correspondence and require self-duality: they apply to L(½, π) with π ≅ π̃, i.e. at t = 0 (or quadratic twists). For t ≠ 0, L(½ + it, ·) = L(½, · ⊗ \|·\|^{it}) is not self-dual and admits no square formula. At t = 0 the corpus already has WD (centre results, row 222). Under RH, c_{2j}(t) = Σ_S Ξ_S(t)² with Ξ_S = Ξ/Π_{γ∈S}(t − γ) is a sum of squares, but its terms need the real zeros. An arithmetic period realization of these Ξ_S without knowing the zeros is the spectral-factorization problem (rows 77/108(b)). | — | Arithmetic square positivity is the right TYPE (exact, product, ℤ-specific, at σ = ½) but exists only at self-dual points (t = 0). Extending square formulas off the self-dual point is a precise form of the missing principle, with no known mechanism. |
| 224 | WD as central-derivative positivity in the Eisenstein family (sharpens rows 2/223). ζ(s+it)ζ(s−it) = L(s, E_t), the standard L-function of the self-dual Eisenstein series E_t = E(·, ½+it), so the slice is g_t(x) = Λ(½+x, E_t) (completed). Hence c₀(t) = Λ(½, E_t) = Ξ(t)² (a square, row 223), and WD ⟺ every even central derivative Λ^{(2j)}(½, E_t) ≥ 0 for all t ⟺ GRH for the family {E_t}. Conjecture: an arithmetic mechanism for positivity of higher central derivatives applies. | STRUCTURAL LINK; OPEN OVER ℚ (paper). The known mechanisms for central-derivative positivity are arithmetic-geometric. Gross–Zagier: L′(½, f) = c·(Néron–Tate height of a Heegner point) ≥ 0, first derivatives only. Yun–Zhang (function fields, unramified PGL₂): the r-th central derivative equals a self-intersection number of Heegner–Drinfeld cycles on moduli of shtukas; positivity would need a Hodge-index-type input there. Over ℚ no higher Gross–Zagier formula is known (no shtukas, r ≥ 2 open), and Eisenstein series need a continuous-spectrum version. | — | Precise arithmetic target behind WD: a higher Gross–Zagier/Yun–Zhang formula over ℚ for the Eisenstein family E_t, expressing Λ^{(2j)}(½, E_t) as a self-intersection or height, plus a Hodge-index positivity. That is the function-field mechanism (row 88) in central-derivative form, absent over ℚ and outside WD. |
| 225 | Arithmetic geometry over ℚ for the row-224 target. Available over ℚ: Kudla's program (central derivatives of incoherent Siegel–Eisenstein series = arithmetic degrees of special cycles; Kudla–Rapoport–Yang, Li–Zhang's proof of the Kudla–Rapoport conjecture) and the Faltings–Hriljac arithmetic Hodge index theorem on arithmetic surfaces (the Arakelov analogue of the Hodge index used in Weil's proof). Conjecture: these supply the higher-derivative positivity for Λ(½, E_t) at every t. | MISSING OBJECT (paper; corpus F₁ no-go ULF-F1-003, Frobenius torsion/Arakelov lanes). Kudla-type formulas concern specific (incoherent) Eisenstein series at special points and first derivatives, not the family E(·, ½+it) at all t nor higher derivatives. Faltings–Hriljac applies to arithmetic surfaces over Spec ℤ, while the surface needed for ζ itself (Weil's C × C analogue) is "Spec ℤ ×_{F₁} Spec ℤ", which does not exist (corpus F₁ forced-shape no-go). The Hodge-index positivity is available exactly where it is not needed. | — | The arithmetic-geometric tools that work over ℚ (Kudla, Faltings–Hriljac) act on genuine arithmetic surfaces and special Eisenstein series. The object WD needs (a self-product of Spec ℤ, or a higher Gross–Zagier for E_t) is absent, consistent with the corpus F₁ and Arakelov no-gos. |
| 226 | Eisenstein analogue of Gross–Zagier via CM points (row 224). For the Eisenstein family, quadratic twists give L(s, E_t ⊗ χ_D) = ζ_K(s+it)-type Dedekind/Epstein products (K = ℚ(√D)), and Kronecker's limit formulas express their values and derivatives at CM points through log\|η(τ_Q)\| (Dedekind η), an exact arithmetic formula with Arakelov-height meaning (Faltings height of CM elliptic curves via Chowla–Selberg/Colmez). Conjecture: a Kronecker-type formula at the centre s = ½ expresses the central derivatives of Λ(s, E_t) as heights, giving WD. | EDGE-ONLY (paper). Kronecker's first and second limit formulas are at s = 1 (equivalently s = 0 by the FE), the pole/edge of the Epstein zeta, not at the centre. Chowla–Selberg/Colmez give Faltings heights through L′(0, χ_D)/L(0, χ_D), again edge values. No limit formula is known at s = ½ for Epstein zetas; their central values have no sign (Epstein zetas can vanish or turn negative at ½ for suitable forms, Davenport–Heilbronn-type). The CM/height structure lives at the edge. | — | The Eisenstein analogue of Gross–Zagier exists only at the edge (Kronecker/Chowla–Selberg/Colmez at s = 0, 1). A central (s = ½) arithmetic-height formula for the Eisenstein family is exactly the missing object of rows 224/225. |
| 227 | Family statistics of central derivatives (row 224 reformulation, t as family parameter). For the Eisenstein family {E_t}, WD ⟺ Λ^{(2j)}(½, E_t) ≥ 0 for all t and j. Iwaniec–Sarnak/Kowalski–Michel-type theorems give positive proportions of nonvanishing or of definite sign for central values and derivatives in families (one-level densities with restricted support, Katz–Sarnak symmetry types). Conjecture: one-level-density positivity in the t-family yields WD for all t. | μ-TYPE / BANDWIDTH (paper; rows 168/186). One-level densities are averages over the family with test functions of restricted Fourier support (support < 2 in the best cases), giving positive proportions, never all members. The Eisenstein family's one-level density is the averaged slice of rows 50–55 [L] (Husimi), already sharp. All-members statements need the full support, i.e. GRH for the family, the target. | — | Family-statistics results (proportions, one-level densities) are μ-type with restricted support. In the Eisenstein family they reproduce the averaged WD results already in Lean and stop there. |
| 228 | Why WD is easy at t = 0 for ζ, and does the mechanism move? On the real axis, ζ has no zeros in (0, 1) by the alternating-series positivity η(σ) = (1 − 2^{1−σ})ζ(σ) = Σ(−1)^{n+1}n^{−σ} > 0 (monotone terms): an exact, Euler-type, pointwise positivity at σ ∈ (0, 1), t = 0. For L(s, χ_D) the same real-axis statement is the absence of Siegel zeros (open: Landau–Page/Siegel, ineffective). Conjecture: a twisted alternating-series positivity (η(σ+it) or Σε_n n^{−σ−it} with suitable signs ε_n) gives pointwise positivity at t ≠ 0. | FAILS OFF THE REAL AXIS (paper). Alternating-series positivity needs real, monotone, alternating terms. For t ≠ 0 the terms (−1)^{n+1}n^{−σ}e^{−it log n} rotate, and η(σ+it) has zeros in the strip (those of ζ, plus 1 − 2^{1−s} = 0 on Re s = 1). Any fixed sign pattern ε_n gives a Dirichlet series whose values for t ≠ 0 are dense-type (Bohr, row 219). The t = 0 positivity is the special real-axis instance of row 219's Re ζ > 0 (here via η). The Dirichlet analogue (no Siegel zeros) is open, showing that even t = 0 WD is hard without the special η-structure of ζ. | — | The only pointwise positivity of ζ inside the strip is the real-axis η-trick (t = 0), specific to the trivial character and non-transportable to t ≠ 0. For characters even t = 0 is the Siegel-zero problem. |
| 229 | Kernel form of WD at height t and Hudson rigidity. With F(z) = ∫Φ(u)e^{zu}du, g_t(x) = \|F(x+it)\|² = ∫e^{xp}K_t(p)dp, K_t(p) = ½∫Φ((p+q)/2)Φ((p−q)/2)cos(tq)dq (a Wigner-type transform of Φ). WD at t ⟺ all even moments ∫p^{2j}K_t(p)dp ≥ 0. At t = 0, K₀ ≥ 0 trivially (positive moments b_k of Φ, corpus centre results). Conjecture: K_t(p) ≥ 0 pointwise for all t, p (which would give WD at every height). | FALSE BY HUDSON-TYPE RIGIDITY (paper; corpus Husimi lane rows 50–55 [L] cites the Hudson/Lütkenhaus–Barnett threshold; row 77). Pointwise nonnegativity of K_t for all t means q ↦ Φ((p+q)/2)Φ((p−q)/2) is positive-definite for each p, i.e. a nonnegative Wigner-type distribution for Φ, which forces Gaussian-type kernels (Hudson). Φ is not Gaussian, so K_t takes negative values for some (t, p). WD survives only as moment positivity of a signed K_t. | — | WD is necessarily a MOMENT-level positivity of signed kernels K_t (pointwise kernel positivity is impossible by Hudson rigidity), consistent with the Husimi sharpness (averaging in t restores pointwise positivity exactly at the Gaussian threshold). Moment positivity of signed kernels has no general certificate (rows 77/96/108). |
| 230 | Hermite/Laguerre coordinates for the signed kernel K_t (row 229; corpus `HermiteL2Expansion` [L], row 56 Hermite-moment identity). Expand Φ = Σa_n h_n in Hermite functions (Φ even, so only even n). Wigner-type transforms of Hermite functions are Laguerre functions (W_{h_n} ∝ (−1)^n L_n(2r²)e^{−r²}, with off-diagonal cross-Wigner terms given by associated Laguerre functions), so each WD coefficient becomes a quadratic form c_{2j}(t) = Σ_{m,n} a_m a_n Λ^{(j)}_{mn}(t) with explicit Laguerre-type kernels. Conjecture: the matrices Λ^{(j)}(t) are positive semidefinite for all t, j, giving WD for every source. | FALSE (paper). PSD for all sources would give WD for every even source with exponential moments, contradicting the Euler-blind countermodel (row 81 [L]: a Gaussian-cosine source with a Husimi zero, and sources whose transforms have off-line zeros). So Λ^{(j)}(t) is indefinite, and WD is the statement that ζ's specific coefficient vector (a_n) lies in the positivity region of every Λ^{(j)}(t), with no structural reason. Hermite coordinates make WD an explicit, infinite family of quadratic inequalities on (a_n), not a positivity theorem. | — | In any orthonormal coordinates the WD coefficients are indefinite quadratic forms in the source coefficients (Euler-blindness forces indefiniteness). WD = membership of ζ's source vector in an intersection of non-convex quadric regions. Arithmetic must decide membership. |
| 231 | Arithmetic membership test (row 230). Φ = Σ_n w_n T_{−log n}φ₁ symmetrized (row 140: translates of the first theta atom), and in Hermite coordinates translation is a displacement operator: ⟨T_cφ₁, h_k⟩ = Σ_m ⟨φ₁, h_m⟩D_{km}(c), with D_{km}(c) explicit (Laguerre polynomials in c²/2 times e^{−c²/4}). So ζ's source vector is a_k = Σ_m⟨φ₁,h_m⟩·Σ_n w_n D_{km}(log n). Conjecture: membership of (a_k) in the WD quadric region (row 230) follows from positivity properties of the displacement sums Σ_n w_n D_{km}(log n). | REDUCES TO DIRICHLET SERIES (paper). The displacement sums Σ_n w_n D_{km}(log n) are Gaussian-weighted Laguerre transforms of the arithmetic measure μ = Σ w_n δ_{log n}, the same measure whose Fourier transform is ζ (row 140). Their positivity properties are Fourier-side statements about ζ on lines/strips, the problem itself in another basis. Laguerre factors oscillate in c, so no termwise positivity. | — | Every coordinate system reduces WD membership to transforms of the arithmetic measure Σ w_n δ_{log n}, i.e. to ζ. Change of basis (Hermite, Laguerre, displacement) only redistributes the arithmetic, it does not add positivity. |

WD = positivity of even central derivatives of Λ(s, E_τ) (row 224); the missing arithmetic object is a central-derivative height formula for this family (rows 224–226). |
| 233 | L-function form of the quarter edge (row 232 places the slice in the L-function world). Conjecture: for a self-dual L-function of degree d with analytic conductor ≍ t^d at height t (e.g. Λ(s, E_t) with d = 2, conductor ≍ t²), the central derivatives Λ^{(2k)}(½) are positive for k ≥ (d/8 + ε)·log(conductor) = (d/4 + ε)·... in t-units, with GRH ⟺ positivity below the edge. The corpus proof uses only the saddle in the Euler region (Gamma-factor curvature ≍ d log t, |log L| bounded for Re ≥ 1 + η) and convexity on the far arc, both available for any degree-d L-function with an Euler product and convexity bound. So the edge sits at the order where the saddle meets Re s = 1, proportional to log(conductor). The general statement is a uniform reformulation family (GRH ⟺ low-order central-derivative positivity), with the same double wall (Euler edge and convexity) for every member. | — | WD's sharp window is an instance of a general L-function law: high central derivatives are positive beyond an Euler-edge order ∝ log(conductor). Below it, positivity is GRH for that L-function. Not formalized (needs a general L-function framework); no new content for ζ. |
| 234 | Scattering formulation for the Eisenstein family (row 232: E_t lives on the continuous spectrum). The scattering matrix of the modular surface is φ(s) = ξ(2s−1)/ξ(2s), with poles at s = ρ/2, so RH ⟺ all scattering resonances on Re s = ¼ (Lax–Phillips, Pavlov–Faddeev: contractivity of a semigroup on a subspace). Conjecture: the WD slice positivity at height t is the positivity of a scattering/Lax–Phillips quadratic form in the E_t-channel, and Lax–Phillips contractivity can be proved from the hyperbolic geometry. | RECEIVER IN CORPUS (searched: `riemannHypothesis_iff_thetaCuspHawkingModularCharacteristicContractive`, KMS–Cayley/Stratonovich scattering-mixing endpoints [L]). The corpus has several scattering/contractivity reformulations of RH as endpoints, without producers. Geometric contractivity of the Lax–Phillips semigroup on the full space is automatic, but on the cusp-form-free subspace relevant to the resonances it is equivalent to RH (Lax–Phillips): the geometry controls the cuspidal spectrum (Selberg), not the Eisenstein resonances, which are arithmetic (ξ(2s)). | — | Scattering theory on the modular surface encodes the zeta zeros as Eisenstein resonances. Contractivity in that channel is RH-equivalent, and the corpus owns several such endpoints. WD's Eisenstein reformulation (row 224) lands in the same receiver family. |
| 235 | Selberg trace formula for PSL₂(ℤ) as a Weil-type identity containing the zeta zeros (row 234). Its spectral side has the cusp sum Σ_j h(r_j) (real r_j, since λ₁ > ¼ for PSL₂(ℤ), no exceptional eigenvalues) and the Eisenstein term −(1/4π)∫h(r)(φ′/φ)(½+ir)dr, which contains ξ′/ξ(2s) and hence the zeta zeros (at ρ/2). The geometric side (identity, hyperbolic, elliptic, parabolic) is explicit. Conjecture: for test functions h ≥ 0 with ĥ ≥ 0, the cusp sum is ≥ 0 and the geometric side is computable, which forces a sign on the Eisenstein (zeta) term, a Weil positivity for ζ. | CIRCULAR / NO CONTROL (paper; corpus HB route hub: Eisenstein/Maass–Selberg sign laws). The Eisenstein term's sign is forced only if the cusp sum is controlled from above pointwise in h, but Σh(r_j) is known only through the trace formula itself (Weyl law plus error), so isolating the Eisenstein term needs independent cusp-spectrum information at the precision of the zeta zeros. With h of compact Fourier support the identity reduces to known Weil positivity at small support (rows 90/185). Large support requires control of Σh(r_j), which is as hard as the zeta term. | — | The trace formula couples cusp spectrum and zeta zeros, but positivity of the cusp side does not transfer a sign to the zeta side without independent cusp control. Same bandwidth/support limitation; corpus HB lane owns this. |
| 236 | Quantum unique ergodicity for the Eisenstein family (rows 224/232: the slice is Λ(·, E_t)). Luo–Sarnak: \|E(z, ½+it)\|²dμ equidistributes (with total mass ∼ (48/π)log t) as t → ∞, a ∀-large-t statement for the same family, proved via Rankin–Selberg/Watson–Ichino triple products and subconvexity. Conjecture: QUE-type control of E_t at every large t transfers to positivity of the central derivatives of Λ(s, E_t), i.e. WD at every large height. | SIZE, NOT SIGN (paper). QUE is an asymptotic equidistribution (main term + o(main term)) proved from subconvex upper bounds on triple-product L-values L(½, φ × E_t × E_t) for fixed φ, a different family evaluated at the centre. It controls sizes of these values, never signs of derivatives of L(s, E_t) at s = ½. The ∀-large-t quantifier comes with an o(1) error, so no exact pointwise sign follows. Same as row 101 (pointwise inputs are upper bounds). | — | Even the strongest ∀-large-t theorems about the Eisenstein family (QUE, subconvexity) are size statements with o(1) errors. Signs of central derivatives need exact positivity (rows 223/224), which QUE technology does not produce. |
| 237 | Connect row 232 to the corpus slice itself. | PROVED [L] (`EisensteinSliceLSeries.xiSliceProduct_eq_gamma_mul_LSeries_eisenstein`: for real x > ½, ξ(½+x+it)ξ(½−x+it) = G_t(x)·Σ_n a_t(n)n^{−(½+x)}, G_t(x) = Π_± s_±(s_± − 1)/2·Γ_ℝ(s_±), s_± = ½ + x ± it; via `xi_one_sub`, `xi_eq_mul_completedRiemannZeta`, `riemannZeta_def_of_ne_zero`). Reformulation anchor, no new positivity. |
| 238 | Sesquilinear (autocorrelation) form of WD from the Eisenstein factorization (rows 224/237 [L]). Since Λ(s, E_t) = ξ(s+it)ξ(s−it) is a product of a GL(1) function and its conjugate, for real x the slice is \|F_t(x)\|² with F_t(x) = ξ(½+x+it) = Σ_a f_a(t)x^a, so c_n(t) = Σ_{a+b=n} f_a(t)·conj(f_b(t)), the autocorrelation of the Taylor coefficients of ξ at ½+it. At a critical zero γ this gives the "rank-2 Gross–Zagier" trivially: Λ″(½, E_γ) = 2\|f₁(γ)\|² ≥ 0 (the central zero of order 2 of a product has positive second derivative). Conjecture: the autocorrelation structure makes every c_n(t) ≥ 0 (a GL(1)×GL(1) degeneracy that trivializes higher Gross–Zagier for Eisenstein series). | FALSE beyond the lowest order (paper; rows 96/108(b)). Autocorrelations of complex sequences, c_n = 2Re Σ_{a<n/2}f_a f̄_{n−a} + \|f_{n/2}\|² (n even), are not sign-definite in general: (f * f̄)_n ≥ 0 for all n is a spectral-positivity condition on the generating function F(x)F̄(x), equivalent to its zeros lying on the imaginary x-axis, i.e. RH. The degeneracy trivializes only the lowest nonvanishing coefficient at each height (c_{2k} = \|f_k\|² when f_0 = … = f_{k−1} = 0), the mixed-class mechanism of row 44 [L]. | — | Eisenstein degeneracy: the GL(1)×GL(1) structure makes the lowest nonzero central derivative a square (trivial rank-r Gross–Zagier), but higher ones are autocorrelations with no sign. WD ⟺ autocorrelation positivity of ξ's Taylor data at every height, which is spectral factorization again. |
| 239 | Rankin–Selberg square of the Eisenstein family as a positivity source. L(s, E_t × E_t) = ζ(s)²ζ(s+2it)ζ(s−2it)/ζ(2s) (Ramanujan's identity) is the L-function of a "square", typically positive in the Euler region (row 123). Conjecture: positivity of L(s, E_t × E_t) or of its completed central data controls the central derivatives of Λ(s, E_t), hence WD. | DEGENERATE / EDGE (paper). At s = ½ the denominator is ζ(1) = ∞ (pole), so the completed Rankin–Selberg function has a zero/pole structure dictated by ζ(2s) at the centre, and its central "value" is 0 or ill-defined rather than a positive square. Its positivity is a Euler-region (σ > 1) property, where the Maass–Selberg/Rankin–Selberg positivity lives (row 123), not a central statement. Its zeros include those of ζ(s+2it), so it encodes the same unknown. | — | The Rankin–Selberg square of the Eisenstein family is degenerate at the centre (ζ(2s) pole) and positive only at the edge. It adds no central positivity for Λ(s, E_t). |
| 240 | Milestone (rows 201–239). Lean: `XiDirectMixedMultiplicityLaw` extended (diagonal coefficient, m = 9 enlargement, row 208); `EisensteinSliceLSeries` (Eisenstein Hecke coefficients' L-series = ζ(w − iτ)ζ(w + iτ), and the corpus slice = Gamma factors × Eisenstein L-series on x > ½; rows 232/237). Paper: θ = 1 crossings (Kloosterman, Estermann, spectral, chaos, length doubling: rows 201–204/213), quasicrystals (205/206), ergodic (207), Selberg-zeta benchmark (209), operators (210), Herglotz (211), Ward caps (212), information vs positivity split (214), RS Wronskian decomposition (215), positive minima (216/217), exponential-sum positivity principles (218), Bohr density (219), hypertranscendence vs jet-cone invariance (220/221), exact identities at ½ (222), Waldspurger squares (223), Eisenstein central-derivative reformulation and higher Gross–Zagier target (224–227), η-trick (228), Hudson rigidity (229), Hermite coordinates (230/231), scattering (234), trace formula (235), QUE (236), autocorrelation/Eisenstein degeneracy (238), Rankin–Selberg square (239). | SUMMARY | — | New precise target (rows 224/232/237 [L]): WD ⟺ positivity of all even central derivatives of the completed Eisenstein L-function Λ(s, E(·, ½+it)) for every t. Arithmetic mechanisms exist only at the edge (Kronecker/Chowla–Selberg), at self-dual points (Waldspurger), for first derivatives (Gross–Zagier), or in function fields (Yun–Zhang). A higher Gross–Zagier formula over ℚ for the Eisenstein family is absent. |
| 241 | Two-variable form of the target (row 240). Λ(s′; s) := ξ(s′ + s − ½)ξ(s′ − s + ½) is the completed L-function of E(·, s), symmetric under s′ ↦ 1 − s′ and s ↦ 1 − s. WD ⟺ ∂_{s′}^{2j}Λ(½; s) ≥ 0 for Re s = ½ and all j. Conjecture: derivatives in the spectral parameter s of E(z, s) at special points (Kudla's program, where s-derivatives of suitable Eisenstein series are generating series of arithmetic heights) supply positivity of the s′-derivatives through the symmetry. | MISMATCHED VARIABLES / OUT OF REACH (paper). Kudla-type height formulas concern s-derivatives of specific (incoherent, Siegel or weight-3/2) Eisenstein series at s = 0, producing generating series in a modular variable, not s′-derivatives of the standard L-function of E(·, s) at s′ = ½. The symmetry s′ ↔ s is not a symmetry of Λ(s′; s) (it exchanges the roles of ξ-arguments s′ + s − ½ and s′ − s + ½ only up to the FE), and positivity of height pairings would need the missing higher Gross–Zagier for the continuous family (row 224). | — | The two-variable picture identifies WD with central s′-derivatives along the critical line in s. Existing height formulas live in the other variable (s at the edge) and other Eisenstein series. No transfer. |
| 242 | Is row 240's target (higher Gross–Zagier for the Eisenstein family) well-posed? Height formulas (Gross–Zagier, Yun–Zhang, Beilinson–Bloch, Kudla) express central derivatives of MOTIVIC L-functions as height pairings of algebraic cycles. Conjecture: Λ(s, E_t) admits such an interpretation for every t. | ILL-POSED FOR GENERIC t (paper, structural). For real t ∉ {0} the series E(·, ½+it) has Laplace eigenvalue ¼ + t², generally transcendental, and Λ(s, E_t) = ξ(s+it)ξ(s−it) is a shift of the motivic ζ by a transcendental amount. It is not the L-function of a motive (no algebraic Hecke eigenvalue field, no Galois representation), so no algebraic cycles, heights or Hodge index can interpret its central derivatives. The only motivic member is t = 0 (ζ², the centre, already known: corpus centre results). | — | Sharpened conclusion: arithmetic-geometric positivity (heights, Hodge index) can only act at motivic special points, i.e. at t = 0 for WD. WD at generic heights must come from a GLOBAL statement over all heights at once (Weil-type explicit-formula positivity, rows 90/185) or a non-motivic analytic principle. It cannot come from pointwise-in-t height formulas. This retires the row-240 target as stated and redirects to global (all-t) positivity. |
| 243 | Global all-t positivity inside WD (row 242's redirection). Candidates: integrate the WD coefficients over all heights against weights w(t), giving finite-part Weil functionals of the zeros (row 79), or impose WD at all heights simultaneously through a single generating object (e.g. ∫c_{2j}(t)e^{−εt²}dt as ε → 0). Conjecture: some global WD functional is provably nonnegative and implies WD at every height. | REDUCES TO WEIL POSITIVITY (paper; rows 79/90/185/186). Global WD functionals are polynomials in Weil-linear functionals (power sums) integrated over t. With compactly supported ŵ they reduce to the archimedean term (small-support Weil positivity, already corpus), and with unbounded support they need the full Weil cone (⟺ RH). A global nonnegative functional implies pointwise WD only through openness plus nullity (row 169) or through the full cone, both RH-strength. | — | Inside WD, every global positivity is Weil positivity in disguise. So the only global principle WD can use is imported from the Weil/CCM lanes (rows 88/136), and WD cannot produce it. Confirms row 88's verdict from the global side. |
| 244 | Probabilistic form of no-slack (row 131 [L]). Model Ξ by random real entire functions f with the same growth and covariance structure (Gaussian analytic functions with covariance K(t, t′) matching the averaged Ξ²; or random theta-type kernels Φ_ω = Φ + noise). By Kac–Rice, the expected density of real zeros of such f is strictly below the total zero density unless K is degenerate (Edelman–Kostlan phenomenon), so almost every member of any non-degenerate random model has non-real zeros, and WD fails at a positive density of heights. Conjecture: some arithmetic random model has WD with positive probability. | FALSE for non-degenerate models (paper; consistent with rows 130/131/154). Real-rootedness is a measure-zero (degenerate) property in every non-degenerate Gaussian model of entire functions of order 1. Φ + generic noise loses LP (corpus damping no-gos, row 154). So RH/WD is a non-generic property of Ξ, invisible to any argument that applies to a positive-measure class of sources. | — | Probabilistic no-slack: WD holds only on a measure-zero set of sources, and Ξ must be singled out by an exact arithmetic identity. Generic-source arguments (random models, perturbative, averaged) are excluded, matching rows 81/131/146/148. |
| 245 | Which exact identity singles out Ξ (row 244)? Hamburger's theorem (corpus [L], memory hamburger-theorem-lean): a Dirichlet series with ζ's functional equation and growth conditions is a constant multiple of ζ. So the pair (FE, Dirichlet series with coefficient structure) characterizes ζ exactly. Conjecture: a Hamburger-type rigidity argument, which characterizes ζ by FE + Dirichlet structure, also forces its zeros to be real (zeros as rigid invariants of the characterization). | NO IMPLICATION (paper). Characterization theorems determine the function, not the location of its zeros: Hamburger's proof matches Mellin transforms and the theta FE and is silent about zeros. Hamburger-type characterizations of Dirichlet L-functions and Epstein-type functions with FE exist too, and Epstein zetas violate the critical line (row 148), so "characterized by FE + Dirichlet structure" does not imply real zeros in general. For ζ the distinguishing feature beyond Hamburger's hypotheses would be the Euler product. That is Hamburger + Euler = ζ again, whose zeros are the question. | — | The exact identity singling out Ξ is known (Hamburger [L] plus Euler product), but characterization carries no zero-location content. RH is a property of the characterized object that the characterization does not reveal. |
| 246 | Prime zeta function form. P(s) = Σ_p p^{−s} = Σ_{k≥1} μ(k)k^{−1}log ζ(ks) continues to Re s > 0 with logarithmic branch points at ρ/k (k squarefree) and a natural boundary at Re s = 0 (Landau–Walfisz). Since log ζ(ks) is analytic on Re s > ½ for k ≥ 2, RH ⟺ P is analytic on ½ < Re s < 1 apart from s = 1. P has positive coefficients. Conjecture: Landau-type positivity for P (row 145) or real-axis analyticity of P on (½, 1) (ζ has no real zeros there, row 228) forces analyticity in the whole strip ½ < Re s < 1. | LANDAU OBSTRUCTION AGAIN (paper; row 145). Landau's theorem for the positive-coefficient series P puts its abscissa at the first real singularity, the logarithmic singularity at s = 1 (from ζ's pole), so convergence stops at Re s = 1 and gives nothing in the strip. Removing it, P(s) − log(1/(s−1))-type corrections, destroys coefficient positivity (as with Λ(n) − 1, Littlewood oscillation). Real-axis analyticity (η-trick) does not propagate off the axis (row 228). | — | Prime-zeta and log ζ formulations meet the same Landau/Littlewood barrier as row 145: positive coefficients pin the abscissa to the pole at 1, and pole removal costs positivity. |
| 247 | Bohr–Jessen and Hilbert spaces of Dirichlet series. For σ > ½, log ζ(σ + it) has a limiting distribution in t (Bohr–Jessen), the Euler product "converges" in the Besicovitch/almost-periodic sense, and ζ(s + ½ + ε) lies in the Hardy space H² of Dirichlet series (Hedenmalm–Lindqvist–Seip), where Nyman–Beurling/Báez-Duarte cyclicity is RH-equivalent (corpus Fermi Nyman–Beurling lane, Báez-Duarte both directions [L]). Conjecture: the almost-periodic Euler product on σ > ½, combined with H² structure, yields pointwise WD positivity. | μ-TYPE / CORPUS RECEIVER (paper). Bohr–Jessen distributions and almost-periodic convergence are statements about the distribution in t (μ-column), and their support is all of ℂ for ½ < σ ≤ 1 (Bohr density, row 219), so no pointwise sign. H² cyclicity (Nyman–Beurling, Báez-Duarte) is an RH-equivalent receiver owned by the corpus (`baez-duarte-criterion-both-directions`), without a producer. | — | Almost-periodic and Hilbert-space-of-Dirichlet-series structures are distributional or receiver-type. Their positivity content is the averaged or the RH-equivalent one, already mapped. |
| 248 | Centre-only data suffice for RH (Pólya): Ξ ∈ LP ⟺ every Jensen polynomial J^{d,0}(X) = Σ_k C(d,k)γ_k X^k of the centre Taylor sequence γ_k (Ξ(t) = Σγ_k t^{2k}/(2k)!·(−1)^k) is hyperbolic. Griffin–Ono–Rolen–Zagier: for each d, J^{d,n} (shifted by n) is hyperbolic for all large n. So RH lives at t = 0 in "all degrees d" rather than at all heights. Conjecture: WD's centre machinery (corpus Turán, PF₃₇, log-concave Taylor coefficients at the centre [L]) extends to hyperbolicity of J^{d,0} for all d. | CORPUS FRONTIER, NO UNIFORMITY (paper; corpus `xi_centre_logConcave`, `xi_centre_turanSeq`, PF₃₇). Hyperbolicity of J^{d,0} for d ≤ 2 is Turán/log-concavity [L]; PF_k of the centre sequence gives hyperbolicity information up to degree ≍ k (corpus PF₃₇). GORZ's large-n result does not cover n = 0 uniformly in d, and the d → ∞ limit at n = 0 is RH. The corpus centre lane already records the reach (PF₃₇, order-23 trace) and that pushing further needs low-height zero information (rows 34/39). | — | Pólya's centre criterion trades heights for degrees: WD at all heights ⟺ Jensen hyperbolicity at t = 0 in all degrees. The corpus centre results are the finite-degree part, and the all-degree uniformity is RH. Same problem, centre coordinates. |
| 249 | Uncertainty principles for the pair (Φ, Ξ). Φ decays doubly exponentially (∼ exp(−πe^{2\|u\|})), Ξ(t) ∼ \|t\|^{7/4}e^{−π\|t\|/4}. Hardy/Beurling-type uncertainty and Beurling–Malliavin zero-set theory constrain how fast a function and its transform can both decay and where an entire function's zeros may lie given its growth. Conjecture: the extreme decay of Φ, via uncertainty, forces the zeros of Ξ onto the real axis (zeros off ℝ would require extra decay or growth incompatible with Φ's tail). | NO ZERO-LOCATION CONTENT (paper). Uncertainty principles bound decay and growth (sizes), and Beurling–Malliavin controls zero DENSITY (completeness radii) for functions of exponential type, while Ξ has order 1 and infinite type in the strip directions. Off-line zeros cost nothing in growth: moving a zero off ℝ by δ changes \|Ξ\| on lines by bounded factors (Hadamard factor ratio), invisible to decay bounds. The doubly-exponential decay of Φ fixes Ξ's order/type (and gives the corpus super-exponential kernel lemmas), not its zero positions (Davenport–Heilbronn and theta-power countermodels have the same decay class, row 148). | — | Uncertainty and zero-density theory see sizes and counts, not the real/non-real location. Same blindness as counting (rows 112/166). |
| 250 | Milestone (rows 241–249). Main structural result of this stretch: row 242, the Eisenstein-family height target is ill-posed at generic heights (E(·, ½+it) is non-motivic), so arithmetic-geometric positivity can act only at t = 0, and generic-height WD needs a GLOBAL principle, which inside WD reduces to Weil positivity (row 243). Supporting closures: two-variable Kudla mismatch (241), probabilistic no-slack (244: WD is a measure-zero source property), Hamburger characterization carries no zero content (245), prime-zeta/Landau (246), Bohr–Jessen/H² (247), Pólya–Jensen centre criterion trades heights for degrees (248), uncertainty blindness (249). No new Lean in 241–249. | SUMMARY | — | Final shape of the WD frontier: (i) detection complete [L]; (ii) reformulations anchored in Lean (Eisenstein L-function, rows 232/237); (iii) producer must be global (all heights at once), exact, Euler/ℤ-specific, rigid at λ = 0 [L] and non-generic (measure zero), i.e. a Weil-type positivity that WD can only import. |
| 251 | Reverse direction: can WD's unconditional results help the Weil lane it would have to import from (row 250)? WD positivity holds on density ≥ 3/4 of heights [L] and the off-line moment is bounded, Σ_{T<γ≤T+H}(β−½)⁺ ≤ H/8-type [L]. Conjecture: for a Weil test function h with ĥ ≥ 0 analytic in a strip, W(h) = Σ_ρ ĥ(γ_ρ) splits as an on-line projection (≥ 0) minus an off-line correction ≤ C·Σ_ρ δ_ρ·sup\|ĥ′\|, so the WD density/off-line-moment bounds give W(h) ≥ 0 for new supports. | ONLY W(h) ≥ −Cμ (paper; corpus Weil-cone memories: summable excess, perturbation rigidity). The correction term is bounded by the off-line moment, which is a positive multiple of T (row 104), so the bound is W(h) ≥ −C·μ·‖ĥ′‖ with μ > 0, never W(h) ≥ 0. Exact Weil positivity needs the off-line moment to vanish, i.e. RH. The corpus Weil-cone lane records the matching rigidity (positivity is not stable under perturbations of size ≍ the off-line moment). WD density statements give Weil positivity up to an O(μ) defect, not exactly. | — | WD → Weil transfer stops at an O(μ) defect, mirroring row 104 (WD density ≥ 1 − O(μ)). Neither lane can lift the other across the exact-positivity boundary. |
| 252 | Bootstrap between the two O(μ) bounds (rows 104/251). WD density ≥ 1 − 2μ and Weil defect ≥ −Cμ both depend on the off-line moment μ. Conjecture: a feedback loop, "WD positivity on good heights ⇒ smaller μ ⇒ larger good set ⇒ …", drives μ → 0 (a contraction argument on μ). | NO FEEDBACK MAP (paper; rows 170/198). μ is bounded from the mollified mean square via Littlewood's lemma, an input independent of where WD holds. The reverse map (good heights ⇒ smaller μ) would need a lower bound on the bad measure per off-line zero, which does not exist (row 170: bad intervals can be arbitrarily short for near-double or shadowed configurations). Near-line zeros, which carry all of μ (row 198), are exactly those with arbitrarily short bad intervals. No contraction exists. | — | The two density lanes (WD bad measure, off-line moment) are linked one way only: μ ⇒ bad measure. Without a per-zero bad-measure lower bound, which near-line configurations violate, there is no bootstrap. |
| 253 | Partial per-zero bad-measure bound (repairing row 252's missing map). For a near-line off-line pair z = γ ± iδ (δ ≪ 1/log γ), order-1 WD at t = γ + d has c₂/c₀ = 2(d² − δ²)/(d² + δ²)² + Σ_{other z′}Re(t − z′)^{−2}, and the pair term is ≈ −2/δ² at d = 0, far larger than the typical critical sum ≍ (log γ)². Hence the direct slice is bad on \|t − γ\| ≲ δ unless another zero lies within O(δ) of γ. Conjecture: this gives B ≥ c·Σ_{unshadowed near-line}δ_ρ, and with B ≤ ε(θ)T, an improved bound on near-line off-line zeros, starting the row-252 bootstrap. | NO GAIN (paper). The resulting bound Σ_{unshadowed}δ_ρ ≤ ε(θ)T/c is weaker than the off-line moment bound Σδ_ρ ≲ μT already used to bound B (row 104): the map just returns its input. The exceptions, near-line pairs shadowed by a critical zero within O(δ) (a "triple near-collision" of three zeros within ≪ 1/log γ), are exactly the configurations whose rarity would require triple correlation of zeros below the spacing scale, beyond the bandwidth wall (rows 186/200). | — | Per-zero bad measure is ≍ δ for unshadowed near-line pairs and uncontrolled for triple near-collisions. Bounding triple near-collisions is a sub-spacing correlation problem, the θ = 1 wall again. No bootstrap. |
| 254 | Correction to row 253 (shadowed near-line pairs are NOT uncontrolled). For δ ≪ 1/log γ, zeros at distance ≫ δ contribute ≍ (log γ)² ≪ δ^{−2} to the slice weights near γ, so the local model of the zeros within O(δ) governs. In that model the direct slice is bad on a window of width ≍ δ: exact mixed class √(2/3)δ (row 111 [L]), multiplicity-m companion ≥ δ√(2/(m+2)) [L] and ≍ δm^{−1/4} (rows 177/178), off-centre companion e ≤ √2δ (at t = γ either the v- or the v²-coefficient is negative). The lower bound B ≳ Σδ_ρ/m_ρ^{1/4} has the same order as the upper bound B ≤ 2Σδ_ρ ≲ μT: both sides scale like the off-line moment, so there is no contraction, only B ≍ μT up to cluster factors. The corrected picture: WD's bad measure is comparable to the near-line off-line moment, and reducing either needs the long-mollifier input. | — | Two-sided law (paper): bad measure of WD ≍ off-line moment Σδ_ρ (up to local-cluster factors). WD density and the off-line moment are the same quantity at this level. A density-one WD theorem is equivalent to off-line moment o(T), the mollifier problem (row 104). |
| 255 | WD density through the mean of log\|ζ\| on the line (follow-up to row 254's two-sided law). Littlewood's lemma: 2πΣ_{0<γ≤T}(β − ½)⁺ = ∫₀^T log\|ζ(½+it)\|dt − ∫₀^T log\|ζ(σ₁+it)\|dt + O(log T), with the σ₁ = 2 integral O(1)·T^{0}. So the off-line moment is (1/2π)∫₀^T log\|ζ(½+it)\|dt + O(log T), and by row 254, WD bad measure ≍ off-line moment. Conjecture: Selberg's central limit theorem (log\|ζ(½+it)\| has mean 0 in distribution) gives (1/T)∫₀^T log\|ζ(½+it)\|dt → 0, hence WD at density 1. | OPEN IN EXACTLY THIS FORM (paper). The CLT gives convergence in distribution, not of the mean: the integral needs uniform integrability of the NEGATIVE tail of log\|ζ(½+it)\| (log-singularities at zeros, near-collisions). Jensen/mollifier bounds control only the positive side and give ∫log\|ζ\| ≤ (T/2)log(mollified mean square), the row-104 bound with ε(θ) > 0. Mean zero of log\|ζ\| on the line ⟺ off-line moment o(T) ⟺ WD density 1 (paper equivalence), which needs the long mollifier. | — | Clean equivalence chain: WD density 1 ⟺ off-line moment o(T) ⟺ (1/T)∫₀^T log\|ζ(½+it)\|dt → 0. The obstruction is the negative tail of log\|ζ\| on the line, controlled only through mollified second moments (θ = 1 wall). |
| 256 | Correction to row 255's diagnosis. Since the off-line moment equals (1/2π)∫₀^T log\|ζ(½+it)\|dt + O(log T) and is ≥ 0, the relevant side is the UPPER bound on ∫log\|ζ\| (large values), not the negative tail: the negative tail only lowers the integral. Upper bounds come from Jensen/mollifiers, ∫log\|ζ\| ≤ ∫log\|ζM\| ≤ (T/2)log((1/T)∫\|ζM\|²) (using ∫log\|M(½+it)\|dt ≥ 0 by Jensen for the Dirichlet polynomial M). The equivalence chain of row 255 stands: WD density 1 ⟺ off-line moment o(T) ⟺ (1/T)∫log\|ζ(½+it)\| → 0. The obstruction is an o(1)-precision upper bound for the mean of log\|ζ\| on the line, i.e. mollified mean square → 1 (θ → ∞, rows 104/201). Distributional (CLT) information is a scale √(log log T) too coarse. | — | The mean of log\|ζ(½+it)\| at precision o(1) is the single analytic quantity governing WD density. Its upper bound is the mollifier problem, its lower bound ≥ −O(log T/T) is free (Littlewood), and RH ⟺ it is O(log T)/T together with exactness. |
| 257 | Low moments as k → 0 (an alternative to mollified second moments for row 256's quantity). Jensen gives (1/T)∫log\|ζ(½+it)\| ≤ (1/2k)log((1/T)∫\|ζ(½+it)\|^{2k}) for every k > 0, and low moments satisfy (1/T)∫\|ζ\|^{2k} ≍_k (log T)^{k²} (Heap–Radziwiłł–Soundararajan, 0 < k < 1). As k → 0, (1/T)∫\|ζ\|^{2k} = 1 + 2k·(1/T)∫log\|ζ\| + O(k²·Var), so the first-order coefficient of the low moment at k = 0 IS the mean of log\|ζ\|. A bound C_k = 1 + o(k) is equivalent to the target. Every low-moment method is sharp only up to k-dependent constants, insufficient at first order. |

Convexity propagates bounds outward from the line, not inward (row 151), and the shape of I_T on (½, 1) is exactly the zero-density profile ∫_σ N(σ′, T)dσ′, which is what is unknown. Convexity yields only I_T(σ) ≤ (1−σ)/(1−½)·I_T(½)-type interpolations, bounds in terms of the unknown value at ½. | — | The log-mean profile σ ↦ I_T(σ) is the integrated zero-density function, and convexity is compatible with any off-line moment at ½. | 300 | Pivot survey after the heat-lane closure, plus C300: an exact detection-window law. For a nonnegative background P, order k of (1 − w/b²)²P is negative iff b² lies in the window of centre p_{k−1}/p_k and half-width √(p_{k−1}² − p_k p_{k−2})/p_k. Exponential regime: \|k − sb²\| < √k. Re-checked mechanism classes: (a) the Wigner/ambiguity form, in which c_{2k}(t) is positive-definite in t with transform M_{2k} ≥ 0; (b) the Laplace–Descartes rule, #off-line zeros on the fibre ≤ sign changes of the ambiguity density m_t; (c) the Rankin–Selberg product T(t,x) = S(0,x)S(2t,x), which carries FE ∧ Euler ∧ Λ≥0 ∧ tempered, unlike S(t,·) for t ≠ 0, whose log-coefficients 2Λ(n)cos(t log n) are signed; (d) symmetric powers of E_t, products of ξ at heights (n−2j)t, as a Frobenius-power family. | C300 REDISCOVERED: rows 282–284 already formalized it (`OffLinePairDetectionMaclaurinBound`, `OffLinePairDetectionNewtonSharp`, `SincBackgroundDetectionWindow`). Module `HTGeometry/DoubleRootDetectionWindows` written, compiled and then deleted, not wired. (a) is the corpus row 77/194 restatement: Bochner positivity of M_{2k} ⟺ c_{2k} ≥ 0. (b) is classical and weaker than zero counting. (c) also detects near-line pairs, since [x²]T ∝ c_2(γ) + 0.023 c_0(γ) < 0; Λ ≥ 0 acts only in the Euler region, so this is CANNOT-470. (d) is killed by the Deligne amplification obstruction: zeros of ζ are not Satake parameters. | none (deleted) | The lane's own header is confirmed: no non-RH-strength pointwise WD statement is open. New observation: the slice L-function L(s,E_t) satisfies only three of the four kill-standard ingredients at t ≠ 0, since Λ ≥ 0 fails. So a WD producer at height t cannot use the slice's own Euler product positively. It must import ζ's positivity, through \|E_t\|² (Rankin–Selberg) or through Weil-type forms. Process lesson: grep the ledger's PROVED rows for the mechanism's key words before writing Lean. |
| 301 | Import ζ's positivity through \|E_t\|² (the four-ingredient object). Conjecture: the positive Parseval density F_t(y) = a₀(y) − \|c(y)\|² = Σ_n \|a_n(y)\|² ≥ 0 of \|E(z,½+it)\|², whose Mellin transform is ξ(s)²ξ(s+2it)ξ(s−2it)/ξ(2s) (Ramanujan's Σσ_aσ_b and ∫K_{it}²u^{s−1} = 2^{s−3}Γ(s/2)²Γ((s±2it)/2)/Γ(s)), has a continuation across the strip that keeps a positive part controlling S(2t,x) at x = 0. Second test: a Weil margin μ₀(A) > 0 at small aperture lowers λ_WD below 1/12. | FAILS (paper). (a) The Mellin integral of F_t ≥ 0 has abscissa 1 (the log-term of F_t as y → 0), so positivity only acts for x > ½. Zagier's truncated continuation gives ∫_{F_T}\|E_t\|²E*(z,½+x)dμ + T-terms. In it E*(z,½+x) = P_z(x) − 4/(1−4x²) with P_z = ∫_1^∞(Θ_z−1)u^{−½}2cosh(x log u)du/u (positive coefficients) against a negative polar part that wins at high order, and E*(i,½) ∝ ζ(½)L(½,χ₄) < 0, so no sign survives. (b) The central coefficients of Λ_RS are not WD: S(2t,x) = Λ_RS(½+x)·ξ(1+2x)/ξ(½+x)², and 1/ξ(½+x)² has alternating coefficients. (c) The Weil form at aperture A < 2 cannot separate ĝ(½+δ+iγ) from ĝ(½−δ+iγ) (ratio e^{±Aδ}), so it constrains no displacement δ and cannot lower the λ_WD per-pair bound δ² < ¼. | none | Λ ≥ 0 reaches WD only through objects whose positive representation lives in the Euler region (Landau). The Rankin–Selberg square changes the WD question (extra ξ(s)²/ξ(2s)) instead of answering it. Lesson: every positive integral representation available (Parseval, theta, Epstein) has a polar part of the opposite sign carrying the Euler-region singularity, and the centre is decided by the competition. |
| 302 | Height window of an off-line pair. Model f(z) = ((z−u)²+δ²)·sin(Lz/2)/(Lz/2) (critical background of density L/2π, pair at height distance u from the slice). Conjecture: the slice criterion fails only at \|u\| ≍ √(δ/L), the geometric mean of the displacement and the spacing, not on the whole exposure cone \|u\| < δ. | PROVED [L]. Exact sign law for m ≥ 2: c_m < 0 ⟺ (D+U−P)² + 4UP < P(8m+2), with D = (δL)², U = (uL)², P = (2m+1)(2m+2). The offset adds 4UP. Order one: c_1 < 0 ⟺ (D+U)² < 24(D−U). Window: (i) any negative coefficient ⟹ (uL)² < 2δL + 6; (ii) δL ≥ 8 and (uL)² ≤ δL − 9 ⟹ some negative coefficient (the bracketing order P_m ≤ D+U < P_{m+1}, m ≥ 3, with U ≤ 2m − 5 and (8m+10)² ≤ 22P). | `HTGeometry/SincBackgroundHeightWindow` (`hasSum_sincOffsetSliceCoeff`, `sincOffsetPairSlice_eq`, `sincOffsetSliceCoeff_add_two_eq`, `sincOffsetSliceCoeff_neg_iff`, `sincOffsetSliceCoeff_one_neg_iff`, `sq_mul_lt_of_sincOffsetSliceCoeff_neg`, `exists_sincOffsetSliceCoeff_neg`). Standard axioms; wired after `SincBackgroundDetectionWindow`. | Order m probes displacement 2m/L at height resolution √m/L, so the detection order δL/2 sees the pair only within √(δ/L) of its ordinate. For δL = O(1) the window is the cone; for δL ≫ 1 it is narrower by √(δL). WD-bad measure is therefore Σ_ρ min(δ_ρ, √(δ_ρ/L)) in the model. Near-line zeros dominate it, so the density constants do not change. This is a model law: in the actual ξ the large-scale count at scale δ carries the O(log t) fluctuation of S(t), which can move the window when δ ≍ 1. |
| 303 | Order–resolution no-go. From row 302, order m resolves displacement 2m/L at height resolution √m/L. Conjecture: no slice data of order ≤ K at a single height can decide an isolated pair with δ < 1/(KL). Side test: the genus-one benchmark. For E/F_q, Λ(½+w) = 2cosh(ℓw) − c, with ℓ = log q and c = a/√q. | (main) FALSE (paper). At the ordinate the pair is a double positive root of g_γ, and c_2(γ)/c_0(γ) = −2/δ² + s < 0 once δ²s < 2 (`XiLaguerreSeparation`, row 44). Order 1 detects every isolated near-line pair at its ordinate. The √m/L resolution limits the height window (row 302), not detection. (side) PROVED on paper: the order-one coefficient of \|2cosh(ℓ(x+it)) − c\|² is 2ℓ²(2 − c cos ℓt). So first-order WD at every height ⟺ \|c\| ≤ 2 ⟺ Hasse, and order 1 already carries the whole criterion in genus one. Under Hasse (c = 2cos ψ) it is the sum of squares (1 − cos(ℓt − ψ)) + (1 − cos(ℓt + ψ)). Point counts N_n ≥ 0, including over twists, give only \|a\| ≤ q + 1. | none | Detection is never the difficulty: positivity production is. The genus-one case shows what the producer must look like. WD order 1 at all heights is exactly Hasse's inequality, its known proofs go through the positive degree form deg(m − nφ) = \|m − nα\|² on End(E) (an eigenvalue, hence a sum of squares), and slice and point-count data do not reach it. The ζ analogue of the first Laguerre inequality is a Hasse-type inequality that needs a degree-form (Rosati-type) positivity. This confirms row 88 at the lowest order. |
| 304 | Genus-g benchmark. Conjecture: first-order WD at every height suffices in genus one (row 303) but not from genus two on, and the needed order grows with the local zero count. Model: Λ(½+w) = Π_j(2cosh(ℓw) − c_j). | TRUE (paper), but REDISCOVERED in substance. For c₁ = 2.1 > 2 > c₂ = 1.95 the order-one coefficient h(X) = (2 − c₁X)(2X − c₂)² + (2 − c₂X)(2X − c₁)², X = cos ℓt, is > 0 on [−1,1]. The first term is negative only for X > 0.952, where it is ≥ −2.5·10⁻⁴ while the second is ≥ 5·10⁻⁴. RH fails (real zeros w = ±arccosh(1.05)/ℓ), and order one at every height does not see it. The corpus already has the direct-slice order-one countermodel (`HTGeometry/FirstLaguerreNotRealRooted`) and the reciprocal one (row 10). | none | Genus one is the only case where order one is complete: a single pair cannot be shadowed. From two pairs on, a near-coincident on-line pair (c₂ ≈ 2) shadows an off-line pair (c₁ ≳ 2) at low order. This is the function-field form of the double-shadowing law. ζ has a local count ≍ log t, which is why its WD window reaches orders ≍ log t (rows 9, 16, N49). |
| 305 | Rankin–Selberg "Siegel" reformulation. D_t(s) = Σ τ_{it}(n)² n^{−s} = ζ(s)²ζ(s+2it)ζ(s−2it)/ζ(2s) has nonnegative coefficients, and on real σ ∈ (½,1), D_t(σ) = ζ(σ)²\|ζ(σ+2it)\|²/ζ(2σ) ≥ 0. Conjecture: Landau–Page/Siegel-zero technology for positive-coefficient families excludes real zeros of D_t in (½,1), i.e. off-line zeros of ζ at height 2t. | CIRCULAR at the wrong edge (paper). The exact statement is new as phrased: RH ⟺ no D_t has a real zero in (½,1) (a "Siegel-zero-free" family). But Landau/Page/Deuring–Heilbronn arguments run on the pole at s = 1 and give only zeros within c/log(t) of σ = 1 (the 3-4-1 product is this family). Siegel zeros of ζ_K = ζL(χ_D), also positive-coefficient with Euler product and FE, are not excluded unconditionally even near 1. | none | Off-line zeros of ζ at height 2t become real zeros of a positive-coefficient Rankin–Selberg family. Positivity of coefficients excludes real zeros only where the series converges, and the classical exceptional-zero technology reaches only the c/log neighbourhood of the pole. This is the Landau obstruction again (row 246). Process: 305 rows confirm the lane-closure verdict (rows 83/88/132): WD has no internal producer. |
| 306 | Two structure-half candidates. (a) Pólya-kernel mixture: Riemann's Φ(u), as a function of λ = cosh 2u, is completely monotone, i.e. a positive mixture of the Pólya kernels e^{−a cosh 2u} whose transforms K_{iz/2}(a) are real-rooted. (b) Interlacing families / finite free convolution (Marcus–Spielman–Srivastava): Ξ-approximants as mixed characteristic polynomials or ⊞_d-convolutions of per-prime real-rooted blocks. Real-rootedness comes free in both. | (a) FALSE (paper). The tail Φ ≈ 2π²(2λ)^{9/4}e^{−2πλ} is log-concave in λ, while completely monotone functions are log-convex. Positive mixtures of Pólya kernels have no common interlacer either, since the shifts u ↦ u + log n multiply transforms by unimodular phases n^{−iz}; that is the Dirichlet series itself. Pólya's theorem is in the corpus (`HTGeometry/BesselOrderPolyaRealZeros`). (b) KILLED (paper). Not in the corpus, but circular without an arithmetic construction: the class is (closed under limits) the real-rooted class, so approximability is RH by MUST-1266. Euler factors (1 − p^{−s})^{−1} are zero-free, so root-set operations (products, ⊞_d) have no per-prime blocks to start from; this is the contractive-local-factor lesson. | none | Pólya's theorem is the Dirichlet spectrum of the Liouville operator −∂_s² + a²e^{2s} on a half-line, a self-adjoint mechanism. Riemann's Φ is a phase-mixture over u-shifts, not an a-mixture, so no common-interlacer or completely-monotone argument applies. MSS-type structure needs per-prime real-rooted blocks, and ζ's local data contain no zeros. |

| 310 | Collision count under the de Bruijn–Newman flow. n(λ) = number of complex zero pairs of H_λ: finite for λ > 0 (KKL), non-increasing in λ, zero for λ ≥ Λ ≤ 1/8 [L]. RH ⟺ n ≡ 0 on (0, ∞). | 312 | Modular input into WD. The slice is the Hecke L-function of E_t = E(·, ½+it), which has full SL₂(ℤ) modularity: additive twists Σ τ_{it}(n)e(an/c)n^{−s} with Voronoi/Estermann functional equations, triple products, and the pre-trace formula ∫h(t)\|E(z,½+it)\|²dt ≥ 0. (b) Distinct t are orthogonal spectral parameters; their only couplings are Plancherel, triple products (ζ-products whose positivity is \|·\|² of values) and ⟨\|E_t\|²,\|u_j\|²⟩ (central values of cusp-form L-functions, not ζ at ½). (c) The pre-trace positivity is pointwise in z. The slice is the geodesic period ∫_0^∞E(iy,½+it)y^x dy/y, and Cauchy–Schwarz from \|E\|² positivity gives only upper bounds on \|slice\|². The Rankin–Selberg square identity needed for the C305 reformulation is already in the corpus (`LSeries_d4BMDivisorProductArithmetic_eq_fourZetaQuotient(_closed)`). | none | Modularity of E_t supplies the slice's functional equation, twist relations and spectral orthogonality in t. None of these is a positivity at the central point. Positivity reaches the slice only as \|E_t\|² (pointwise, or Rankin–Selberg in the Euler region), so modularity alone gives WD no producer. This matches the corpus reading that zero information must enter through modularity in a positive form not yet identified, which is the HF twin door (C329/WP07, the only open problem next to the producers that is not RH-strength). |

Strip edges: by averaging (window majorant + box bound), every [n, n+1] contains a zero-free height T with ∫_{½}^{2}\|ξ'/ξ(σ ± iT)\|dσ ≤ B_n = O(log n). Enclosed zeros exhaust {Re ρ > ½+θ}. | 315 | Row 42's open question: a WD-native "Γ_n", i.e. a family of slice functionals indexed by prime powers whose positivity bounds the Laguerre power sums uniformly in height. Two candidates. (A) Twist the Laguerre zero sum by prime ratios: L_y(t) = −Σ_ρ y^{ρ−s₀}/(ρ−s₀)², s₀ = ½+it, and products K_P(ρ−it) of cosine factors (the JAS kernels with Laguerre weight). With the cosine products K_P the family is linear in the zero measure with a kernel positive on the line; RH ⟺ positivity for all (P, t) (P → ∞ unmasks every off-line zero because Σ_p p^{δ−1} diverges). This is the JAS cosine-kernel family with the Laguerre weight instead of 1/(ρ(1−ρ)). | 327 | Does Lindelöf (μ = 0) give every-height heat positivity at every rate κ > 0, i.e. κ_WD = 0? (1) Exact tilt identity: with A_t(σ) := S(t, σ−½)·t^{−(σ−½)}, H_λ(y) = t^{y+κ/2}·E[A_t(½+y+κ+√λZ)] for λ = κ/log t. So heat at rate κ/log t is a horizontal shift by κ plus smoothing at width √(κ/log t). (2) Size-only methods are limited by two walls. (i) κ > ½: the positive window must lie where S^{(N)} ≥ 0 is known, i.e. in the Euler region. (ii) A parabola condition on the Lindelöf function, (κ−x)²/(2κ) > 2μ(½+x) for x ∈ [0,½]. With the interior bound ζ(5/7+it) ≪ t^{1/12} (corpus k=4 test) it gives about 0.71; with the sharp k=4 bound t^{1/14}, about 0.675; the limit is 4μ(½) = 2/3. Lindelöf gives ½. (3) The truth is Θ-blind at every κ > 0. In the cosh model the heated even coefficient of order n ≈ (δ−κ)L is ∝ P(δ) − P''(δ)/(2L) + P(2κ−δ) + …, and the mirror term P(2κ−δ) is of order 1, so the model stays positive for 0 < κ < δ as well as κ ≥ δ (the corpus proved κ ≥ δ). (4) Heuristically, failure at rate κ < ½ needs a horizontal drop of the smoothed log ζ(σ+it)² by ≳ √(κL) over σ-width √(κ/L), i.e. a cluster of ≳ √(κL)/log L zeros within √(κ/L) of the saddle. Jensen with any known μ (and Backlund under LH) allows clusters of size o(L), far above this. So LH alone does not decide κ_WD < ½. | none | The heat lane is closed as a producer lane. Above ½ it is a subconvexity lane, bounded below by 4μ(½) for size-only methods. Below ½ it is a mesoscopic clustering question, independent of Θ (cosh blindness) and not settled by Lindelöf. Neither regime touches the Θ-core or the low-order signs. The only unconditional gain left is in constants via interior Lindelöf points (0.76 → ≈0.71 → 2/3). That is incremental and not pursued. |

| 368 | Synthesis after rows 356–367: where WD stands. | STATE. (i) Receiver theory is complete up to constants and now has a per-configuration table in Lean: isolated pair b²/3, pair behind an order-m critical zero ≥ 2b²/((m+2)(2m+3)) (rows 358, 362, 364), with dBN analogues b²/2, b²/6 and the parity law t_m (row 365). (ii) Comparison λ_WD ∈ [Λ/2, Λ] (row 329) survives every finite configuration tested and is built into both size-only methods (row 366); for ξ it needs the transfer that row 328 identified as Θ-core-hard. (iii) A single off-line zero has only local WD consequences: for \|t − γ\| > b its slice factor x⁴ + 2((t−γ)² − b²)x² + ((t−γ)² + b²)² has positive coefficients. Any global contradiction from one off-line zero must therefore come through the prime side (Landau oscillation x^{β}), i.e. wall 1. (iv) Every wall is now tested from WD: 1, 2, 3, 5–10 (rows 343–350), 4 (row 367). The producer is RH-strength in every reading (Θ-core strip + shallow-pair exclusion = uniform local exclusion = √x-precision one-sided prime counting). | none | WD contributes receivers and quantitative dictionaries; a WD proof of RH needs an input that passes wall 1 or 3, and none exists in the corpus. |
